Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 removes two local persistent NativeArray allocation/disposal routes. Sonar reveal pays one generation validation per pulse shell, then writes up to nine grid cells through a local descriptor.

## Decision 063 - Topographical sonar stops persisting pointer-bearing Vault handles
Problem: `TopographicalSonarSynthesizer.cs` persisted eleven legacy `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors for point cloud rows, hit masks, counters, mock SDF/material payloads, 300-frame telemetry, CSV scratch, indirect args, and shader globals. Even without explicit private `NativeArray<T>` fields, the legacy descriptors carried pointer metadata and used `handle.Resolve`, so a Vault relocation could leave a stale lease path in a UI sonar system.
Solution: Replace all eleven descriptors with `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, cache `IDataVault` once through `ResolveDataVaultCold`, resolve method-local views with `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`, and release each exact descriptor during teardown after outstanding scan/fade job fences complete. The shutdown path now decrements Vault references instead of only defaulting local handles.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping legacy handles because the system is visual-only was rejected; visual sonar still schedules Burst jobs and uploads GPU buffers from Vault data. Calling `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` at every resolve site was rejected because the registry is cold identity and dependency injection only.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same continuous ray-count collapse, mock SDF fake, and GPU point rendering. Middle/high/ultra keep denser point clouds, material LUT color, indirect draw args, and shader globals without changing ownership or DTO layout.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays O(1) generation checks at scan/fade/upload/telemetry phase boundaries. Per-ray `SampleTopographicalSonarJob` and per-point fade loops use local `NativeArray<T>` descriptors and do not revalidate handles inside the hot loop.

## Decision 064 - PDA frequency tuning resolves Vault waves through generation descriptors
Problem: `PDADecryptionSpectrogramPanel.cs` persisted six legacy `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors and one shared helper used `handle.Resolve(vault)` for wave rows, GPU segments, stage targets, and the 300-frame telemetry ring. It also cleared descriptors on teardown instead of releasing Vault references.
Solution: Convert the six descriptors to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, make the shared helper validate existing handles through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`, reacquire descriptors through `GetGenerationHandle<T>` when absent or undersized, and release exact descriptors on teardown/DataVault replacement after completing the scheduled wave job.
Rejected Alternatives: Releasing inside the resolve helper after a transient resolve failure was rejected because a compaction fence or temporary Vault unavailability must not decrement ownership from a hot resolve path. Keeping the old helper was rejected because `handle.Resolve` can use stale pointer metadata.
Scalability potential: Low devices continue to collapse the spectrogram point count through continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` and VRAM clamp. Middle/high/ultra keep denser wave segments, GPU tube rendering, and telemetry without changing the minigame truth route.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays bounded generation checks at phase entry. The Burst wave generation/error jobs still receive local `NativeSlice<T>` views and do not validate per point.

## Decision 065 - Babel subtitle runtime stops retaining legacy Vault descriptors
Problem: `BabelSubtitleSyncRuntime.cs` persisted two static `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors and resolved subtitle cue/telemetry buffers through `handle.Resolve`. A static runtime survives scene churn and can keep pointer-bearing metadata alive across Vault relocation or DataVault replacement.
Solution: Replace both descriptors with `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, resolve cue and telemetry buffers through cached `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`, reacquire with `GetGenerationHandle<T>` during cold initialization, and release BufferIDs `15070550` and `15070551` on subsystem reset or DataVault hot-swap after fencing any active cue evaluation job.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the legacy handle because the runtime already used method-local `NativeArray<T>` values was rejected; the persistent descriptor itself still carried pointer metadata. Releasing from ordinary read helpers was rejected because a transient compaction fence must not mutate ownership from a read accessor.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same continuous subtitle dirty budget collapse through `ResolveCanvasDirtyBudget`. Middle/high/ultra keep the same visual-only cue flow and 300-frame telemetry without changing authority, DTO layout, or save identity.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays two generation validations at cue/telemetry phase entry. The Burst cue evaluation job still receives a local pointer after a fresh resolve and performs no per-cue descriptor lookup.

## Decision 066 - CharBufferPool Babel arena removes latest-vault and legacy descriptor routes
Problem: `CharBufferPool.cs` persisted a static `VaultBufferHandle<char>` for the Babel arena and resolved it through `handle.Resolve`. It also used `GlobalDataVault.TryGetLatestCreated()` as a fallback, which is a bootstrap/editor/diagnostic/crash-only route and should not be a UI formatting dependency.
Solution: Replace the Babel arena handle with `VaultGenerationHandle<char>`, resolve method-local `NativeArray<char>` views through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`, acquire with `GetGenerationHandle<char>`, release the descriptor on subsystem reset, and use only `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` for cold Vault injection. When no Vault exists, the existing managed TMP bridge arrays remain the fallback.
Rejected Alternatives: Releasing on every resolve failure was rejected because compaction fences and temporary Vault unavailability must not mutate ownership from a read path. Keeping `TryGetLatestCreated()` was rejected because it hides an implicit global heap/diagnostic dependency in a UI helper.
Scalability potential: Low devices can skip the native arena and use the fixed TMP bridge arrays when the Vault is unavailable. Middle/high/ultra retain the Vault-backed Babel arena without changing text capacity, save identity, or localization authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation validation when a Babel span or native-to-TMP copy uses the native arena. The fallback path avoids Vault lookup churn and uses preallocated bridge arrays already owned by the pool.

## Decision 067 - PDA shell borrows the glitch glyph table instead of owning it
Problem: `PDAShellChrome.cs` persisted a `VaultBufferHandle<byte>` and used `ResolvePointer` against `DiegeticGlitchSurgeonRuntime.GlitchTableBufferIdRaw`. The file also allocated the shared table through `GetBufferHandle` and could fall back to `GlobalDataVault.TryGetLatestCreated()`, making a PDA chrome component an implicit owner of a shared glitch glyph fact.
Solution: Replace the field with `VaultGenerationHandle<byte>`, borrow the existing descriptor with `IDataVault.TryGetGenerationHandle`, resolve method-local `NativeArray<byte>` views through `TryResolveHandle`, and derive the raw byte pointer only for the immediate `GlitchEncoder` call. On invalid/missing table, fall back to the table-free glitch encoder and clear the borrowed descriptor.
Rejected Alternatives: `GetGenerationHandle<byte>` was rejected because it can create a shared glyph table from a non-owner and then force release ambiguity. Releasing on disable was rejected because the PDA shell is now a borrower, not the owner. Keeping `TryGetLatestCreated()` was rejected because it is crash/diagnostic bootstrap-only.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the table-free glitch encoder path when the shared table is absent. Middle/high/ultra use the richer glyph substitution table when the owner has published it, without changing text layout, save identity, or authority route.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation validation only when stress-reactive strings need the table. The no-table fallback avoids Vault allocation and keeps the existing fixed scratch buffer path.

## Decision 068 - UberNoir telemetry ring uses generation descriptors only
Problem: `HectonUberNoirRuntimeBridge.cs` persisted `VaultBufferHandle<UberNoirShaderTelemetryEntry>` for the shader feature blackbox and called `.Resolve(vault)` in push/dump paths. The bridge did not persist `NativeArray<T>`, but the legacy handle itself still carried pointer metadata across Vault relocation.
Solution: Replace the field with `VaultGenerationHandle<UberNoirShaderTelemetryEntry>`, resolve method-local views with `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`, reacquire existing `ShaderFeatureTelemetryRing` through `TryGetGenerationHandle`, allocate only when missing and allocation is unlocked, and release the owner-local descriptor on disable/destroy/DataVault replacement. Compaction-fence paths clear only local metadata and fail closed.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the legacy handle because the ring is graphics-only was rejected; blackbox telemetry is a persistent Vault fact and must be relocatable. Releasing from transient resolve failure was rejected because maintenance fences must not decrement ownership. Adding a private telemetry array fallback was rejected because it would create a second proof artifact.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing continuous UberNoir feature collapse through `GlobalQualityWeight`, stress shedding, and shader feature masks. Middle/high/ultra keep the same visual-overkill feature gating while the telemetry ring remains generation-safe.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one bounded generation validation per telemetry push/dump phase. The ring write/copy loop still uses a local `NativeArray<T>` descriptor and does not revalidate per field.

## Decision 069 - Docking spline service stops exporting cached Vault pointers
Problem: `DockingAutopilotService.cs` persisted `VaultBufferHandle<ActiveSplineData>`, used `ResolvePointer`, and read `_activeSplineHandle.ptr` directly in service methods. These methods are called by vehicle automation code across frames, so a Vault relocation could leave docking slot reads/writes pointed at dead memory.
Solution: Store `VaultGenerationHandle<ActiveSplineData>` plus a cached row count, resolve method-local `NativeArray<ActiveSplineData>` views at each service operation boundary, and remove the manager helper that returned `ActiveSplineData*`. Existing buffer recovery uses `TryGetGenerationHandle`; owner-local creation uses `GetGenerationHandle` only when allocation is unlocked. Disable/shutdown/DataVault replacement release the descriptor through the owning Vault.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping raw pointers for slot scans because the buffer is small was rejected; pointer lifetime safety is independent of capacity. Adding a private active-spline mirror was rejected because docking truth would split into two owners. Reacquiring via `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` inside each service method was rejected because registry access is a cold DI path only.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same bounded 64-slot default and cubic Dear Lie docking curve. Middle/high/ultra can raise the serialized capacity up to 256 without changing DTO layout, authority route, or slot semantics.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation validation per acquire/write/read/evaluate/release call. The O(n) slot scans still operate on a local `NativeArray<T>` descriptor and do not validate the handle per slot.

## Decision 070 - Material decay blackbox ring stops using legacy handle resolution
Problem: `MaterialDecayRuntime.cs` persisted `VaultBufferHandle<MaterialDecayState>` and used `.Resolve(_dataVault)` for the VFX blackbox ring. The runtime is visual, but the ring is still a 300-frame proof artifact and stale pointer metadata can fault after Vault compaction.
Solution: Replace the persisted descriptor with `VaultGenerationHandle<MaterialDecayState>`, resolve method-local blackbox views through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`, recover existing buffers with `TryGetGenerationHandle`, allocate owner-local storage only when the Vault is unlocked, and release the descriptor on disable/destroy/DataVault replacement.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the legacy handle because the telemetry is VFX-only was rejected; proof artifacts must be relocatable exactly like gameplay buffers. Releasing on compaction-fence failure was rejected because maintenance phases must not mutate ownership. Adding a managed/native fallback ring was rejected because it would create a second blackbox authority.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing shader-scalar rust/wetness/blood fake and low-tier flag. Middle/high/ultra keep richer material presentation through the same global shader uniforms without changing DTO layout or signal ownership.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one bounded generation validation per blackbox push/dump phase. The material shader path and rust atlas generation are unchanged.

## Decision 071 - Orbital prologue blackbox stops retaining a NativeArray view
Problem: `OrbitalRelativityDirector.cs` retained both `VaultBufferHandle<OrbitalTelemetryEntry>` and a persistent `NativeArray<OrbitalTelemetryEntry>` telemetry ring. The prologue director can span scene transitions and DataVault replacement, so a compaction or hot-swap could leave the stored ring view pointing at relocated memory.
Solution: Delete the persistent `NativeArray` field, store only `VaultGenerationHandle<OrbitalTelemetryEntry>`, resolve a method-local ring through cached `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle` immediately before record/dump, and release the descriptor on dispose, runtime-authority release, or DataVault replacement. Resolve failures during compaction only clear the local descriptor.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the ring view because telemetry is low frequency was rejected; a stale alias faults regardless of write cadence. Adding a local fallback ring was rejected because it creates a second blackbox truth outside the Vault.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing impostor/orbital math LOD and whiteout Dear Lie. Middle/high/ultra keep richer orbital presentation using the same telemetry facts without changing DTO layout, save identity, or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation validation per telemetry record/dump phase. The 300-entry dump loop uses a local descriptor and does not revalidate per entry.

## Decision 072 - Foveated render blackbox removes raw pointer resolution
Problem: `FoveatedRenderCommander.cs` persisted `VaultBufferHandle<FoveatedRenderTelemetryEntry>` and resolved a raw `FoveatedRenderTelemetryEntry*` through `ResolvePointer`. The commander is a singleton-style VR graphics policy owner, so stale telemetry pointers can survive across XR display sampling, UI suppression passes, DataVault replacement, or Vault compaction fences.
Solution: Replace the field with `VaultGenerationHandle<FoveatedRenderTelemetryEntry>`, resolve method-local `NativeArray<FoveatedRenderTelemetryEntry>` views through cached `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`, stamp entries with `handle.Generation`, and release the descriptor on disable, dispose, or DataVault replacement. The class no longer needs an unsafe context.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping `ResolvePointer` because the ring is graphics telemetry was rejected; blackbox proof artifacts must be relocatable. Calling `TryGetBufferGeneration` was rejected because it adds a second lookup for a value already validated in the generation descriptor.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep existing fixed-foveation pressure escalation and UI fail-closed behavior. Middle/high/ultra keep gaze-tracked or richer foveation policy where supported without changing DTO layout, XR authority, or quality route.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation validation per telemetry write/dump phase. The XR display enumeration and policy application costs are unchanged; the dump loop uses a local NativeArray descriptor.

## Decision 073 - HullDents has one owner and one borrowed repair mutator
Problem: `HullDentShaderController.cs` and `RepairTool.cs` both used legacy `VaultBufferHandle<float4>` routes for `BufferID.HullDents`. Both could allocate or resolve through pointer-bearing handles, and a naive generation migration would let a borrower release another owner's shared visual dent lane.
Solution: Make `HullDentShaderController` the allocator/owner for `BufferID.HullDents=(BufferID)76`, because it owns shader upload and visual dent presentation. `RepairTool` now borrows the descriptor with `TryGetGenerationHandle` and mutates only after a phase-local `TryResolveHandle`. Owner bits gate `ReleaseBuffer`, so descriptors recovered through `TryGetGenerationHandle` are clear-only. `RepairToolBlackBox=(BufferID)340` separately uses an owned generation descriptor and local NativeArray record/dump views.
Rejected Alternatives: Allowing `RepairTool` to call `GetGenerationHandle` for `HullDents` was rejected because it creates refcount ambiguity for a shared visual fact. Keeping raw blackbox pointers was rejected because blackbox proof artifacts are not exempt from relocation safety. Moving capacity/packing into a new shared contract was deferred to avoid a cross-domain contract edit; both sides already use capacity 16.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the cheap 16-entry shader dent fake and low-tier scar scalar. Middle/high/ultra can still feed richer dent shader presentation from the same shared lane without changing payload layout or damage authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation validation per hull dent sync/flush/repair phase and one validation per repair blackbox write/dump. Shader upload remains a fixed 16-vector global array; no CPU mesh deformation is introduced.

## Decision 074 - Camera juice blackbox stops using pointer-bearing telemetry handles
Problem: `CameraJuiceSystem.cs` persisted `VaultBufferHandle<CameraJuiceTelemetryEntry>` and resolved the 300-frame telemetry ring through `handle.Resolve(_dataVault)`. The runtime is camera presentation, but it is singleton-style and participates in hot-swap/service rebinding, so a stale descriptor can survive compaction or DataVault replacement.
Solution: Replace the field with `VaultGenerationHandle<CameraJuiceTelemetryEntry>`, resolve method-local `NativeArray<CameraJuiceTelemetryEntry>` views through cached `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`, and gate `ReleaseBuffer` with `_ownsCameraJuiceTelemetryBuffer`. Recovered descriptors from `TryGetGenerationHandle` are borrowed and clear-only; descriptors acquired with `GetGenerationHandle` are released on disable, destroy, or DataVault replacement.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping `VaultBufferHandle<T>.Resolve` because telemetry is visual-only was rejected; blackbox proof artifacts must remain relocatable. Unconditional release of a recovered descriptor was rejected because it can decrement another owner's Vault reference. Changing the camera-shake math or DTO layout was rejected because this pass is pointer safety, not presentation redesign.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing low-tier sampled shake/FOV scalar collapse. Middle/high/ultra retain procedural camera overkill through the same explicit 64-byte telemetry rows without changing BufferID, save identity, or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation validation per telemetry record/dump phase. The 300-entry dump loop uses a local `NativeArray<T>` descriptor and does not validate per entry.

## Decision 075 - Player cinematic focus proof ring becomes relocatable
Problem: `HectonPlayerMovement.cs` persisted `VaultBufferHandle<CinematicFocusTelemetryEntry>` and used `handle.Resolve(_dataVault)` for the cinematic focus blackbox. Player movement is long-lived, registry-rebound, origin-shift aware, and fault-dump capable; retaining a pointer-bearing descriptor keeps the exact stale-alias failure class open in a player-critical runtime.
Solution: Store `VaultGenerationHandle<CinematicFocusTelemetryEntry>`, resolve method-local `NativeArray<CinematicFocusTelemetryEntry>` views through cached `_dataVault.TryResolveHandle`, and gate release with `_ownsCinematicFocusBlackBox`. `TryGetGenerationHandle` recovery is borrowed and clear-only; `GetGenerationHandle` acquisition is released on teardown or DataVault replacement.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting the whole player kinematics native-state wrapper was rejected as a larger owner lane with separate buffers. Keeping the legacy blackbox handle because it had no persistent `NativeArray<T>` field was rejected because the descriptor itself still carried stale pointer metadata. Changing the cinematic focus DTO or dump format was rejected because the ABI is already explicit 96 bytes.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing cinematic focus pull/fade scalar route and dump cadence. Middle/high/ultra retain richer camera-framing presentation without changing gameplay truth, save identity, or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation validation per focus sample/dump phase. The 300-entry dump loop stays sequential over a local `NativeArray<T>` view and does not validate per row.

## Decision 076 - Suit HUD borrows glitch glyph table instead of owning it
Problem: `SuitHUDV4CanvasOverlay.cs` persisted a `VaultBufferHandle<byte>`, allocated `DiegeticGlitchSurgeonRuntime.GlitchTableBufferIdRaw=70901` through `GetBufferHandle`, fell back to `GlobalDataVault.TryGetLatestCreated()`, and resolved a raw glyph-table pointer. That made a HUD overlay an implicit allocator/writer for a shared UI glyph fact owned by `DiegeticGlitchSurgeonRuntime`.
Solution: Store `VaultGenerationHandle<byte>`, borrow with `IDataVault.TryGetGenerationHandle`, validate with `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`, and derive `byte*` only from the freshly resolved method-local `NativeArray<byte>` for immediate `GlitchEncoder` use. Lifecycle teardown and DataVault replacement clear only the borrowed descriptor. Invalid, missing, or stale tables fail closed into the existing table-free decay encoder.
Rejected Alternatives: Allocating or initializing the shared glyph table from HUD was rejected because it splits ownership and release authority. `GlobalDataVault.TryGetLatestCreated()` was rejected as a bootstrap/editor/crash-only fallback. Caching the derived `byte*` was rejected because Vault relocation can invalidate native addresses between phases.
Scalability potential: Low devices use table-free glitch decay through the existing fixed scratch buffer. Middle/high/ultra use richer shared glyph substitution when the owning glitch surgeon has published the table, without changing HUD text authority, save identity, DTO layout, or quality route.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation validation only when corrupted HUD text needs the shared table. No Vault allocation, no managed allocation, no persisted native alias, and no borrower-side table copy remain.

## Decision 077 - Vehicle docking telemetry stops exporting raw Vault pointers
Problem: `VehicleDockingModule.cs` persisted `VaultBufferHandle<DockTelemetryEntry>` and `VaultBufferHandle<int>`, resolved both with `ResolvePointer`, and returned `DockTelemetryEntry*` / `int*` to record and dump helpers. The module is pooled and multi-instance, so cached pointer-bearing descriptors can survive DataVault replacement, compaction fences, and dock lifecycle churn.
Solution: Replace both persisted descriptors with `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, resolve method-local `NativeArray<DockTelemetryEntry>` and `NativeArray<int>` views through cached `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`, and make telemetry record/dump write through those local views only. `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` is now read by a cold cache helper instead of the hot resolve path.
Rejected Alternatives: Releasing `VehicleDockingTelemetryRing` or cursor from `OnDisable` was rejected because this is a shared `SystemID.VehiclesPhysics` blackbox lane and `GetGenerationHandle` does not provide a per-module refcount increment when the BufferID already exists. Borrow-only clear preserves the old process-lifetime ownership while eliminating stale pointers. A private module telemetry ring was rejected because it would create a second blackbox proof artifact.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing 300-row blackbox and low-tier docking spline sampling cadence. Middle/high/ultra retain richer docking diagnostics through the same 128-byte rows without changing docking truth, signals, save identity, or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays two generation validations per telemetry write/dump phase. The dump loop uses one local ring descriptor and one local cursor descriptor; no raw pointer is retained between phases.

## Decision 078 - Loot magnet local Vault views use generation descriptors
Problem: `LootMagnetSystem.cs` did not persist legacy handles, but its generic `TryResolveVaultView<T>` still created `VaultBufferHandle<T>` values, called `TryGetBufferHandle`, and resolved through `.Resolve(vault)`. Cleanup helpers also fell back to `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` when cached `_vault` was null.
Solution: Replace the helper with `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, `GetGenerationHandle<T>` for owner allocation phases, `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>` for read-existing phases, and `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle` for method-local `NativeArray<T>` views. Cleanup and unlock paths now use cached `_vault` only; dependency refresh remains the cold registry route.
Rejected Alternatives: Leaving local legacy descriptors because they are not fields was rejected; the migration goal is to eliminate pointer-bearing API use, not just persistent storage. Adding persistent per-lane handles was rejected because this scheduler already has clean method-local view acquisition and does not need more long-lived state.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain the existing cadence collapse through scalability tier and acquisition budgets. Middle/high/ultra keep the same richer pull/acoustic/wake signal output without changing item authority, DTO layout, save identity, or signal route.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays bounded generation validation when lane views are acquired. The per-entity pull, commit, and cleanup loops still operate over local `NativeArray<T>` views and do not revalidate handles per item.

## Decision 079 - Fauna corpse sink kinematics stop using legacy Vault views
Problem: `FaunaBrain.cs` persisted two `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors for one-row corpse-sink kinematic input/output buffers and resolved them through `.Resolve(vault)`. The helper also polled `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` from the schedule/complete boundary, which is active runtime work, not cold injection.
Solution: Store `VaultGenerationHandle<CorpseSinkKinematicInput>` and `VaultGenerationHandle<CorpseSinkKinematicOutput>`, cache `_corpseSinkVault` during lifecycle/spawn, and resolve method-local input/output views through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`. Teardown completes any outstanding job and clears descriptors only.
Rejected Alternatives: Releasing the one-row fauna kinematic buffers from a pooled brain was rejected because the BufferIDs are shared and the current Vault API does not expose per-fauna refcount ownership for existing buffers. Creating private NativeArrays was rejected because corpse-sink job data is rollback/blackbox-adjacent and must remain Vault-backed.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing cheap corpse-sink vertical settle job and death presentation fakes. Middle/high/ultra retain the same whale-fall/corpse presentation data path without changing fauna truth, DTO layout, save identity, or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays two generation validations at corpse-sink schedule/complete boundaries. The Burst job receives method-local one-element NativeArray views and does not retain a Vault alias after scheduling.

## Decision 080 - Visor refraction blackbox stops retaining a raw Vault pointer
Problem: `HectonVisorFluidDistortionFeature.cs` persisted `VaultBufferHandle<VisorRefractionTelemetryEntry>`, used `ResolvePointer`, compared `GenerationID` against `TryGetBufferGeneration`, and polled `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` from the render-pass blackbox path. SRP features are long-lived renderer assets, so cached pointer metadata can survive DataVault replacement or compaction and fault during telemetry write or dump.
Solution: Store `VaultGenerationHandle<VisorRefractionTelemetryEntry>`, cold-bind `_dataVault` from lifecycle/hot-swap routes, resolve method-local `NativeArray<VisorRefractionTelemetryEntry>` views through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`, and gate release with `_blackBoxHandleOwned` plus current generation validation. The ring writer and dump loop no longer require an unsafe context.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the raw pointer because the buffer is render telemetry was rejected; blackbox proof artifacts must be relocatable. Polling `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` from `TryEnsureBlackBoxLease` was rejected because registry is cold DI only. Releasing every recovered descriptor was rejected because renderer assets can bind to a buffer allocated by another owner.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing continuous visor collapse through `GlobalQualityWeight`, thermal motion cull, and homeostasis fallback. Middle/high/ultra keep richer lens/refraction shader presentation through the same telemetry lane without changing BufferID, DTO layout, shader payload, or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation validation at blackbox frame-write/dump entry. The 300-row dump uses a local `NativeArray<T>` descriptor and does not revalidate per row; the visual shader path is unchanged.

## Decision 081 - Screen-space shaft VFX lanes stop using legacy locked resolves
Problem: `ScreenSpaceLightShaftRuntime.cs` persisted three `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors and resolved them through `.Resolve(vault)` after taking write locks. The lock order protected concurrent writers, but the descriptor still carried stale pointer metadata across DataVault replacement or compaction.
Solution: Replace top contribution, history contribution, and telemetry descriptors with `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, cold-bind `_dataVault` through lifecycle/hot-swap routes, and resolve method-local `NativeArray<T>` views with `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle` only after all three buffers are locked. Existing lanes are borrowed; newly acquired descriptors are release-gated by current generation.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping `.Resolve(vault)` because locks were already present was rejected; locks do not repair a stale pointer descriptor. Polling `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` from `EnsureBuffers` was rejected because late-frame VFX work is a hot path. Removing the Vault locks was rejected because this pass changes descriptor safety, not writer synchronization.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing continuous low-tier shader tap clamp, load-shed timer, and brownout triangle fake. Middle/high/ultra keep stronger shaft intensity and richer source selection through the same three-slot contribution lanes without changing DTO layout, shader property IDs, signal route, or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays three generation validations after the existing lock triplet. The max source tracker remains fixed at three 64-byte entries, so contribution sort/history costs stay bounded and cache-local.

## Decision 082 - Scannable lore entity buffers stop caching NativeArray views
Problem: `ScannableTarget.cs` already used `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, but it cached static `NativeArray<AbsoluteUniversePosition>` and `NativeArray<uint>` views, then separately polled `TryGetBufferGeneration` to decide whether the cached views were current. If the Vault relocates, the cached view can still point at dead native memory before the separate generation check protects all call sites.
Solution: Delete the static `NativeArray<T>` view cache and separate generation cache. Every lore entity read/write phase resolves method-local AUP/hash views through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle` using the stored generation descriptors.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping cached views for 1024-slot scanner speed was rejected because descriptor validation is cheaper than a crash and the sync loop remains bounded. Keeping `TryGetBufferGeneration` was rejected because it creates a second route for the same fact.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same 1024-slot lore scanner mirror and title cache. Middle/high/ultra can read richer scanner/lore overlays from the same BufferIDs without changing DTO layout, title route, save identity, or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays two generation validations when lore buffers are read or written. The owner sync loop still walks a fixed array and does not validate per slot.

## Decision 083 - Player kinematics generic binding stops carrying pointer handles
Problem: `PlayerKinematicsRuntime.VaultBufferBinding<T>` stored `VaultBufferHandle<T>` and used `GetBufferHandle<T>` / `Handle.Resolve(dataVault)`. The helper backs fifteen player kinematics SOA lanes, so the pointer-bearing descriptor risk was multiplied across body state, hand probes, telemetry, and SDF squeeze results.
Solution: Store `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, reuse existing handles through `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>` when valid, allocate with `GetGenerationHandle<T>` only when needed, and resolve every view through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`. The helper still returns method-local `NativeArray<T>` views to existing jobs and call sites.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting all player kinematics call sites to explicit view structs was rejected for this pass because the generic helper already centralizes the dangerous descriptor. Adding unconditional release was rejected because the previous helper only cleared views and ownership/refcount changes across fifteen lanes need a separate teardown audit.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing player kinematic math LODs, SDF squeeze clamps, and hand probe budgets. Middle/high/ultra keep the same richer IK/telemetry paths without changing DTO layout, BufferID identity, save state, or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays generation validation when a binding view is read, replacing the prior legacy resolve cost. There is no additional per-element validation inside the player body or telemetry loops.

## Decision 084 - Hazard exposure job result stops using a pointer-bearing handle
Problem: `HazardZoneManager.cs` persisted `VaultBufferHandle<HazardExposureJobResult>` and resolved it through `.Resolve(vault)` after polling `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` inside the result preparation route. The manager is a long-lived gameplay service and its one-row result buffer can cross DataVault replacement or compaction boundaries while the hazard job schedule/consume loop keeps running.
Solution: Store `VaultGenerationHandle<HazardExposureJobResult>`, cold-bind `_dataVault` during lifecycle setup, register a `GlobalRegistry` hot-swap listener, and resolve method-local `NativeArray<HazardExposureJobResult>` views through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`. Allocation-capable `GetGenerationHandle` is limited to cold setup or replacement recovery; hot schedule/consume calls use cached `_dataVault` and existing generation descriptors.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping `.Resolve(vault)` because the result has one row was rejected; stale pointer descriptors are element-count agnostic. Unconditional `ReleaseBuffer` on teardown was rejected because the current hazard job path does not hold a Vault write lock and a disabled manager can still have an active scheduled writer. Moving all hazard registry NativeArrays into the Vault was rejected for this bounded pass because they are Sentinel-registered owner-local scratch and require a separate route card.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing cheap AABB-sphere hazard exposure math, toxicity accumulation, and visor-glitch scalar route. Middle/high/ultra can keep richer hazard presentation from the same 128-byte result row without changing DTO layout, BufferID identity, save state, shader payload, or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation validation at schedule/consume boundaries and removes one service-locator lookup from each result preparation. The Burst job still writes a single 128-byte result row and does not validate per hazard volume.

## Decision 085 - PDA H8LR lore mirror stops retaining a Vault byte pointer
Problem: `PdaH8lrLoreStore.cs` used a `VaultGenerationHandle<byte>` for the H8LR mirror, but after cold file copy it stored `_basePointer` from `NativeArrayUnsafeUtility.GetUnsafeReadOnlyPtr(vaultMirror)` and later guarded it with `TryGetBufferGeneration`. A matching generation check cannot repair a native address that was cached before a Vault relocation or DataVault replacement.
Solution: Keep `_basePointer` persistent only for the memory-mapped-file path. For Vault-backed lore, validation still uses the byte pointer during cold open, then clears `_basePointer`; every lookup resolves `_vaultMirrorHandle` through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle` and derives a method-local byte pointer from the returned `NativeArray<byte>`.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping `_basePointer` because lore records are read-only was rejected; read-only stale pointers still fault. Replacing MMF with Vault-only storage was rejected because standalone/editor MMF is a separate cold file path and not the stale Vault alias. Copying each lore record into a managed string was rejected because PDA text streaming must remain allocation-free.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing H8LR/Babel/mock source priority and B-tree quality-weight lookup. Middle/high/ultra can stream richer PDA text from the same Vault mirror without changing record layout, localization authority, save identity, or quality route.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation validation per H8LR lookup before the B-tree scan. No per-record validation is added inside the B-tree walk, and no managed allocation is introduced.

## Decision 086 - Architect Eye hot entity labels stop splitting view and generation facts
Problem: `ArchitectEyeVisualizer.cs` built hot-entity labels and trails from `TryGetBuffer<VaultHotEntityData>` while separately polling `TryGetBufferGeneration` for a display label. The visual path split one Vault fact into two lookup routes.
Solution: Add `TryResolveHotEntityData`, which borrows the `VaultHotEntityData` generation descriptor with `TryGetGenerationHandle`, resolves the local view with `TryResolveHandle`, and returns `handle.Generation` for the label.
Rejected Alternatives: Patching only the generation label was rejected because adjacent hot-entity reads would still use the legacy `TryGetBuffer<T>` route. Persisting a diagnostic view was rejected because Architect Eye is a visual consumer, not a Vault owner.
Scalability potential: Low devices can keep the existing reduced diagnostic draw density. Middle/high/ultra keep richer Architect Eye overlays from the same hot entity facts without changing DTO layout, shader payload, or authority route.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation validation per visual build phase. Entity label, sector-map, trail, and fallback probe loops use the local view and do not revalidate per row.

## Decision 087 - PDA encyclopedia streamer stops caching Vault NativeArray views
Problem: `PDAEncyclopediaStreamer.cs` had already moved its lanes to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, but it cached a `PdaVaultViews` struct containing persistent `NativeArray<T>` views for ten buffers and guarded those views with `TryGetBufferGeneration`. If the Vault relocates, the cached `NativeArray<T>` may carry the old native address even if later metadata checks detect churn.
Solution: Delete `PdaVaultViews`, `_vaultViewsCached`, the generation-poll helper, and the synthetic `ConvertExistingDataToNativeArray` wrapper. `ResolveVaultBuffer` now returns the actual `NativeArray<T>` from `_vault.TryResolveHandle`; `GetVaultElementRef` derives a transient pointer from that phase-local view only.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the cached views because PDA UI is low frequency was rejected; stale native aliases are cadence-independent. Rebuilding the PDA stream path around managed strings was rejected because it violates the zero-GC text streaming contract. Adding a second local mirror was rejected because localization/lore truth must stay in the Vault/Babel/H8LR routes.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing typewriter Dear Lie, mock lore fallback, and continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` reveal speed. Middle/high/ultra can decode richer H8LR/Babel pages through the same byte-addressed lanes without changing DTO layout, save identity, or localization authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation descriptor validation at each PDA lane access boundary. UTF-8 decode, metadata probe, and telemetry copy loops operate on a resolved local view and do not validate per byte or per character.

## Decision 088 - Respawn reconciliation validates current descriptors by resolving them
Problem: `ShinobuRespawnReconciliationRuntime.cs` used `TryGetBufferGeneration` inside `IsVaultGenerationCurrent`, creating a separate metadata route for handles that are otherwise used through `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` and `TryResolveHandle`.
Solution: Replace the metadata generation poll with `TryResolveVaultBuffer(vault, in handle, out NativeArray<T> buffer)` and require `buffer.IsCreated`. Owner checks and required-length checks remain in the existing wrapper methods.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the metadata poll because it is cheap was rejected; cheap duplicate facts still violate the one-route rule. Removing the generation-current helper entirely was rejected because respawn reconciliation has both owned and borrowed physiology/player kinematic descriptors and still needs an explicit cold/hot fence.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing respawn fade, penalty, and med-bay reconciliation cadence. Middle/high/ultra can retain richer telemetry/proof data through the same descriptors without changing payload layout, save identity, or gameplay authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation descriptor validation per current-handle gate. No per-row validation is added to med-bay, telemetry, CSV, physiology, or kinematic loops.

## Decision 089 - L-System genome editor borrows a descriptor instead of a pointer handle
Problem: `LSystemGenomeLabWindow.cs` used `TryGetBufferHandle(BufferID.FloraGenomeDtos, out VaultBufferHandle<FloraGenomeDTO>)` and `handle.Resolve(vault)` in an editor facade. Editor windows can remain open across Vault reloads, so even editor-only pointer-bearing descriptors keep the stale-alias pattern alive.
Solution: Borrow `BufferID.FloraGenomeDtos` with `TryGetGenerationHandle` and resolve a method-local genome view with `TryResolveHandle` inside `OnGUI`. Missing or empty buffers still use the existing mock preview.
Rejected Alternatives: Ignoring editor code was rejected because Task 17 requires Vault X-Ray/editor facades to model safe handle access. Allocating or populating flora genomes from this window was rejected because flora genome ownership belongs to the flora genome runtime/baker, not the preview UI.
Scalability potential: Low devices are unaffected; this is editor-only. Middle/high/ultra preview still uses the same high-tier L-system preview controls without changing runtime flora DTO layout or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime impact is zero. Editor cost is one descriptor validation per `OnGUI` preview pass.

## Decision 090 - Save delta helpers stop producing pointer-bearing descriptors
Problem: `EntityDeltaGizmoProbe.cs`, `EntityDeltaCompressionArchitecture.cs`, and `VoxelDeltaCompressionArchitecture.cs` still used `TryGetBufferHandle` or `GetBufferHandle<T>` plus `.Resolve(vault)`. Save delta buffers are binary payload routes; stale descriptors here risk corrupting or misreporting WAL/delta state.
Solution: Replace the gizmo read with `TryGetGenerationHandle` plus `TryResolveHandle`; replace both compression architecture helpers with `GetGenerationHandle<T>` plus `TryResolveHandle`.
Rejected Alternatives: Leaving these as cold/editor helpers was rejected because binary save routes are precisely where stale views are least acceptable. Reworking compression or checksum logic was rejected because the defect is descriptor lifetime, not codec math.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same frame-budgeted save/WAL cadence and emergency mock schemas. Middle/high/ultra keep richer save telemetry and larger payload throughput through the same DTOs without changing save identity or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one descriptor validation per helper acquisition or gizmo draw. RLE, checksum, WAL, and gizmo sector loops do not validate per byte or per sector.

## Decision 091 - Visual pressure aging removes duplicate Vault generation metadata route
Problem: `VisualPressureAgingRuntime.cs` stored generation descriptors but still called `TryGetBufferGeneration` in owned buffer validation, initialization recovery, external input validation, and stale-external detection. That made descriptor validity depend on a separate metadata fact before the same handle was resolved.
Solution: Use `TryResolveHandle` through the existing descriptor helpers as the single proof of generation freshness. Keep `IsHandleForBuffer`, `IsExternalHandleValid`, owner checks, and required-length checks unchanged.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping generation metadata polling because it was explicit was rejected; explicit duplicate facts are still route drift. Rebuilding the visual aging Vault layout was rejected because payload identity and shader ABI are already explicit and outside this pointer-lifetime pass.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same low-tier visual aging/degradation collapse and CSV tuning. Middle/high/ultra keep richer UberNoir degradation telemetry and shader upload through the same descriptors without changing quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 removes a metadata poll at validation gates. Aging/degradation jobs, telemetry pushes, and graphics-buffer uploads continue over local views without per-element descriptor checks.

## Decision 092 - Fluid dynamic wake buffers stop carrying pointer handles
Problem: `HectonFluidEngine.cs` persisted `VaultBufferHandle<float4>` descriptors for `WakeGlobalBuffer` and `WakeVectorBuffer`, allocated through `GetBufferHandle`, and resolved with `.Resolve(vault)` immediately before GPU upload. Dynamic wake presentation can run across Vault compaction or DataVault replacement, so pointer-bearing descriptors are unsafe even when the data is visual.
Solution: Store `VaultGenerationHandle<float4>` descriptors, borrow existing wake buffers during allocation-locked phases, allocate with `GetGenerationHandle` only when unlocked, and resolve method-local `NativeArray<float4>` views with `TryResolveHandle` before upload.
Rejected Alternatives: Removing the dynamic wake route was rejected because the shader-side wake fake is the cheap visual substitute for CPU fluid simulation. Releasing the wake buffers on fluid teardown was not added because the prior route only cleared descriptors here and wake ownership is already documented as a multi-owner conflict with VFX lanes.
Scalability potential: Low devices can fail closed to the existing empty wake buffer and low-tier wake params. Middle/high/ultra keep richer dynamic wake GPU advection from the same two 16-slot float4 lanes without changing shader ABI or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays two descriptor validations per dynamic wake upload phase. The upload loop still copies local `NativeArray<float4>` views to the GPU buffer and does not validate per slot.

## Decision 093 - Seed Ship shader slot bridge removes VaultGenerationID cache
Problem: `SeedShipAnomalyShaderBridge.cs` cached `VaultBufferHandle<float4>` plus `vault.VaultGenerationID` for `BufferID.ShaderGlobalState`. The metadata generation cache could say "current" while the pointer-bearing descriptor still represented stale address state.
Solution: Store `VaultGenerationHandle<float4>`, resolve slot views with `TryResolveHandle`, and use `TryGetGenerationHandle`/`GetGenerationHandle` only to acquire the descriptor. Direct shader globals remain as a fallback when the Vault slot lane is unavailable.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping `VaultGenerationID` for a fast cache was rejected because the generation descriptor already carries the proof and must be validated by resolve. Removing direct shader globals was rejected because the anomaly presentation should fail closed visually even when Vault slot allocation is unavailable.
Scalability potential: Low devices can publish the cheap scalar globals only. Middle/high/ultra keep the richer shared shader slot route without changing slot index, DTO layout, or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one descriptor validation per publish path. The bridge writes one `float4` slot and does not validate per shader parameter.

## Decision 094 - Submarine structural breach lanes stop using pointer handles
Problem: `SubmarineStructuralGrid.cs` persisted `VaultBufferHandle<float4>` for active breaches and `VaultBufferHandle<DamageControlTelemetryEntry>` for damage-control blackbox telemetry. Vehicle structural state can run for long sessions, so stale descriptors here would hit both visual breach upload and proof-artifact telemetry.
Solution: Store generation descriptors, resolve method-local views through `TryResolveHandle`, and borrow existing descriptors during allocation-locked phases. Allocation remains limited to unlocked Vault phases.
Rejected Alternatives: Migrating the entire structural grid private native state was rejected for this bounded pass because it includes many owner-local job arrays and requires a separate Vault route card. Keeping the two handle fields because the data is visual/blackbox was rejected because blackbox proof artifacts and long-lived breach visuals must be relocatable.
Scalability potential: Low devices can fail closed on breach VFX/telemetry during Vault maintenance. Middle/high/ultra retain richer breach plume/repair visuals and blackbox evidence through the same BufferIDs without changing DTO layout or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at breach and telemetry resolve boundaries. Breach repair jobs and telemetry loops use local views and do not validate per row.

## Decision 095 - Terrain seam baseline and blackbox lanes stop caching native aliases
Problem: `WorldGenerativeGeologyTerrainSeamApplier.cs` persisted `VaultBufferHandle<float>` per terrain baseline, a `NativeArray<float>` baseline alias in `TerrainApplyState`, and `VaultBufferHandle<TerrainSeamTelemetryEntry>` for the seam blackbox. Terrain apply state can survive multiple reconcile/preview passes, so both the handle and the stored native view could point at relocated Vault memory.
Solution: Store `VaultGenerationHandle<float>` and `VaultGenerationHandle<TerrainSeamTelemetryEntry>` descriptors only. Resolve method-local `NativeArray<T>` views through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle` before baseline copy/populate/projection and blackbox record/dump. During allocation-locked phases, borrow existing descriptors with `TryGetGenerationHandle`; allocate/grow only while the Vault is unlocked.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the baseline `NativeArray<float>` cache because terrain patching is editor/slow-path was rejected; stale native aliases are cadence-independent. Reworking MapMagic, hybrid projection, voxel blend mask upload, or terrain writeback was rejected because the defect is descriptor lifetime, not terrain math. Releasing baseline buffers from `TerrainApplyState.ReleaseBaseline()` was rejected for this bounded pass because existing state did not own reference-count teardown and terrain baseline BufferIDs are per-terrain compatibility lanes.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing low-tier visual-only seam projection path and can fail closed on missing Vault lanes during compaction. Middle/high/ultra keep richer mask detail, blackbox evidence, and shader blend-mask upload through the same BufferIDs without changing terrain payload layout or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation descriptor validation when a baseline or blackbox view is opened. Heightmap sample loops, hybrid Burst jobs, and blackbox dump loops operate on the local resolved view and do not validate per sample or per entry.

## Decision 096 - Submarine fluid wrapper stops carrying raw Vault pointers
Problem: `SubmarineFluidDynamics.cs` used a generic `VaultNativeBuffer<T>` wrapper holding `VaultBufferHandle<T>`, dereferenced `_handle.ptr` in its indexer, refreshed through `TryGetBufferHandle`, and polled `TryGetBufferGeneration`. That one wrapper backed all compartment, bulkhead, hydro, blackbox, and exterior thermal lanes, so a single stale pointer could contaminate the whole submarine fluid route.
Solution: Replace the wrapper storage with `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` plus a cached length scalar. `Ensure` acquires/grows via `GetGenerationHandle<T>` only while the Vault is unlocked and borrows via `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>` under allocation lock. `Refresh`, indexer access, and implicit `NativeArray<T>` conversion open method-local views through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`.
Rejected Alternatives: Patching each of the 28 lane fields separately was rejected because the stale alias pattern was centralized in the wrapper. Keeping a cached `NativeArray<T>` view inside the wrapper was rejected because a `NativeArray<T>` carries a native address and can go stale after relocation. Releasing all fluid buffers on disable was rejected for this pass because the existing route only clears wrapper state after dependency fences and ownership/refcount policy for shared vehicle lanes needs a separate teardown audit.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing fluid math LODs, visual wake/hydro Dear Lie paths, and can fail closed on Vault allocation lock. Middle/high/ultra retain richer hydro blackbox, mass-property, thermal anomaly, and custom-drag behavior through the same BufferIDs without changing quality authority or DTO layout.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation when the wrapper opens a view. Scheduled Burst jobs still receive local `NativeArray<T>` values and do not validate descriptors inside their loops. The stale raw pointer path is removed for all submarine fluid lanes.

## Decision 097 - Flora/fauna symbiosis solver moves to generation descriptors
Problem: `ShinobuFloraFaunaSymbiosisSolver.cs` persisted `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors for all major symbiosis lanes, resolved them with `.Resolve(vault)`, and exposed an unused `GetFloraRef` bridge through `GetElementAsRef`. These buffers feed long-lived AI ecosystem jobs and can cross Vault relocation, so pointer-bearing descriptors are unacceptable.
Solution: Store `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` descriptors for all symbiosis lanes. Add local helpers for claim, borrow, and resolve so cold setup uses `GetGenerationHandle<T>` only when unlocked, allocation-locked phases borrow existing descriptors, and runtime reads/writes derive method-local `NativeArray<T>` views through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`.
Rejected Alternatives: A file-wide managed abstraction was rejected because the hot path needs flat descriptors and local `NativeArray<T>` views. Keeping the byref `GetFloraRef` bridge was rejected because a ref must originate from a current phase-local view. Editing simulation jobs was rejected because their inputs already accept `NativeArray<T>` and the defect was stale descriptor lifetime, not math.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing capped neighbor samples, mock fish fallback, and reduced ecosystem cadence. Middle/high/ultra retain richer scanner VFX, oxygen emitter, adherence, seed, and acoustic tap lanes through the same BufferIDs without changing DTO layout or quality authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays generation validation at view-open boundaries. Flora/fish/link Burst loops still receive local arrays and do not validate per entity or per link.

## Decision 098 - Thermodynamics file worker is blocked until Core exposes a relocation-pinned lease
Problem: `ThermodynamicsHazardGridRuntime.FileWorker.cs` keeps `_binaryConstantsWorkerPtr` and `_csvWorkerPtr` from Vault byte lanes for a background worker thread. The worker can outlive Vault relocation, and the current evidence does not show defrag honoring active writer metadata strongly enough to make direct worker writes safe.
Solution: Do not apply a cosmetic local `TryResolveHandle` patch. The safe migration is to parse binary constants and CSV overrides into worker-local staged scalar payloads, publish staged readiness with `Volatile.Write`, and apply to `_constants` on the owner/main phase after resolving a `VaultGenerationHandle<ThermodynamicsHazardConstants>`.
Rejected Alternatives: Letting the worker resolve a `NativeArray<byte>` locally was rejected because resolution returns a phase-local view, not a relocation-pinned lease. Holding a normal write lock was rejected because the inspected relocation path does not prove it fences external worker writers. Leaving the issue undocumented was rejected because this is a real UAF class.
Scalability potential: Low devices benefit from avoiding worker/Vault contention and can keep cheap staged constants. Middle/high/ultra keep richer thermal hazard tuning once applied from the owner phase; gameplay truth and DTO layout stay unchanged.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 avoids unsafe background writes into relocatable Vault memory. The future safe patch shifts file IO parsing to worker-local memory and one owner-phase descriptor validation before writing constants.

## Decision 099 - Toxic outgassing chemistry removes pointer descriptors and public constants pointer escape
Problem: `ToxicOutgassingChemistryRuntime.cs` persisted `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors for every density, chemistry, signal, telemetry, CSV, binary, and header lane, resolved them with `.Resolve(vault)`, exposed constants through `ConstantsRef`, and exposed a raw pointer through `TryGetConstantsPointer`. These lanes are long-lived, scheduled, and blackbox-visible; stale aliases here can corrupt gas density, chemical exposure signals, and the 300-frame dump.
Solution: Store `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` descriptors only. Open method-local `NativeArray<T>` views with `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle` before owner mutation, job setup, telemetry commit, shader publish, CSV parse, and binary probe. Replace constants ref/pointer facade with value read/write calls and update the editor tuner to edit a local copy before writing it back.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the editor-only pointer was rejected because editor windows can outlive Vault relocation and still model unsafe API usage. Replacing every call with a per-cell generation check was rejected because descriptor validation belongs at phase boundaries, not inside Burst loops. Moving toxicity buffers into private native arrays was rejected because atmosphere chemistry state and blackbox data are Vault-owned proof/state lanes.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain low-resolution radial fallback, continuous quality-weight cadence, and cheap nearest-density sampling. Middle devices keep full scheduled diffusion with lower source budgets. High/ultra retain trilinear sampling, richer mock flow/world sampling, shader scalar publishing, and blackbox detail through identical BufferIDs and DTO layouts.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one descriptor validation at each view-open boundary. The 32^3 diffusion, flow, exposure, biolum, and telemetry Burst jobs still run over local `NativeArray<T>` views with `[NoAlias]` fields and no per-cell descriptor validation. Removing public pointer escape eliminates one relocation UAF class without adding managed allocations.

## Decision 100 - Ambient biota service removes cached Vault handles and read-only aliases
Problem: `AmbientBiotaDirector.cs` persisted six `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors and three `NativeArray<T>.ReadOnly` aliases created through `IDataVault.CreateAlias`. Read-only aliases still carry native addresses, and the ambient service exposes them to chunk residency consumers, so relocation could leave consumers reading stale biota AUP/state memory.
Solution: Store generation descriptors for biota AUP, velocity, state, macro-hydration counter, telemetry ring, and telemetry cursor lanes. Resolve local views with `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle` at phase/service boundaries. Public read-only service properties now return transient `.AsReadOnly()` wrappers over freshly resolved local views rather than cached aliases.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping cached read-only aliases was rejected because read-only does not mean relocation-safe. Reworking the `IAmbientBiotaService` contract was rejected because it is a cross-domain contract; the same return types can be backed by current generation-resolved views. Replacing indirect draw or macro hydration logic was rejected because the defect is descriptor lifetime, not presentation math.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain survival capacity, reduced macro visual hydration, organic debris coalescing, and billboard flags. Middle/high/ultra retain larger capacities, indirect draw payloads, visual overkill flags, and blackbox telemetry through the same BufferIDs and DTO layouts.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one descriptor validation when opening biota/counter/telemetry views. Drift, spawn, hydration, dehydration, and residency loops still iterate local arrays without per-row descriptor validation. Removing `CreateAlias` fields eliminates three persistent native-address aliases.

## Decision 101 - Cartography handle bundle moves to generation descriptors
Problem: `CartographyGridJobs.cs` defined `CartographyVaultHandles` with `VaultBufferHandle<T>` fields, allocated them through `GetBufferHandle`, borrowed through `TryGetBufferHandle`, and opened all cartography views through `.Resolve(vault)`. `PlayerExplorationTracker` persists this bundle, so stale descriptors could hit discovery words, telemetry, tuning, scanner profiles, upload payloads, and rollback snapshots.
Solution: Replace every field in `CartographyVaultHandles` with `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`. Unlocked acquisition now uses `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, allocation-locked acquisition uses `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>`, and `TryResolveViews` opens all buffers through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting `PlayerExplorationTracker` was rejected because the consumer already depends on the bundle API and the stale pointer route is centralized inside `CartographyVault`. Adding per-word descriptor checks was rejected because discovery, RLE, and upload jobs must remain dense data-local loops.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain sparse reveal cadence, packed R8 upload, and surface mask approximations. Middle/high/ultra retain larger visible point budgets, scanner profile tuning, mock exploration data, RLE/debug lanes, and rollback snapshots through the same BufferIDs and DTO layouts.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation once per cartography view binding. Discovery, sonar, POI injection, RLE, upload, telemetry, and rollback Burst jobs continue to operate on local arrays without descriptor checks inside word/voxel loops.

## Decision 102 - Ecosystem balancer removes pointer descriptors from swarm truth lanes
Problem: `ShinobuEcosystemBalancer.cs` persisted `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors for ambient entity/AUP rows, boid states, frame snapshots, sector/tuning/counter/telemetry/debug lanes, render matrices/custom data/indirect args, spatial hash buffers, CSV scratch, legacy scratch, and species profiles. It resolved them through `.Resolve(vault)` and kept an unused `GetAmbientEntityRef` bridge over `GetElementAsRef`, leaving the primary swarm simulation exposed to stale native addresses after Vault relocation.
Solution: Store `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` descriptors only. Add `ClaimVaultHandle` and `TryOpenVaultView` so allocation uses `GetGenerationHandle<T>` only when the Vault is unlocked, locked phases borrow existing descriptors through `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>`, and every owner phase derives method-local `NativeArray<T>` views with `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting flocking, spatial hash, macro biomass, HZB/GPU upload, or predator logic was rejected because the defect is pointer lifetime, not ecosystem math. Keeping the static byref helper was rejected because any ref must originate from a current local view, not a persisted pointer handle. Adding per-boid descriptor checks was rejected because boid loops must remain vectorizable dense array work.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the continuous quality-weight budget collapse, update stride, reduced neighbor samples, and GPU/indirect fail-closed behavior. Middle/high/ultra retain richer flocking samples, debug cells, indirect procedural draw payloads, CSV-authored species profiles, and blackbox telemetry through the same BufferIDs without changing gameplay authority, DTO layout, shader ABI, or save identity.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation only at schedule, CSV, initial-population, GPU-upload, and telemetry boundaries. The local-shift, spatial hash, flocking, render payload, macro biomass, indirect args, and telemetry count jobs still consume local arrays without per-row descriptor validation or managed allocation.

## Decision 103 - Ecosystem population governor borrows external lanes and releases owned descriptors
Problem: `EcosystemPopulationBalancer.cs` persisted eight `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors and resolved them through `.Resolve(vault)`. Six lanes are owned population buffers, while `EntityAUPs` and `EntityFlags` are external truth lanes; treating both as pointer handles risks stale aliases and treating both as owned would violate authority boundaries.
Solution: Convert all eight descriptors to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`. Owned buffers `EcosystemPopulationCoefficients`, `EcosystemPopulationSectorState`, `EcosystemPopulationCullEvents`, `EcosystemPopulationTelemetryRing`, `EcosystemPopulationFreeRing`, and `EcosystemPopulationCounters` use `GetGenerationHandle<T>` only when allocation is legal and are exact-released on teardown/DataVault rebind. External `EntityAUPs` and `EntityFlags` are borrowed through `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>` and resolved only into method-local views.
Rejected Alternatives: Owner-wide `ReleaseOwnerBuffers(SystemID.AIEcology)` was rejected because `SystemID.AIEcology` is shared by other ecology runtimes. Rewriting population math or death SignalBus payloads was rejected because the unsafe route was Vault descriptor lifetime, not the bounded Lotka-Volterra job.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same cold-tick population governor, free-ring reuse, and tier flee-down requests. Middle/high/ultra keep richer sector/cull telemetry and SignalBus death proof through the same DTO layouts; `GlobalQualityWeight`, save identity, and entity truth ownership are unchanged.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays generation validation at cold setup, sector-state rebuild, job schedule, telemetry write, and signal publish boundaries. The Burst job still receives local `NativeArray<T>` values and does not validate descriptors per entity or per sector.

## Decision 104 - Apex brain vault bundle moves to pointer-free descriptors
Problem: `ShinobuApexBrainVault.cs` persisted a public bundle of fifteen `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors, opened all cognition lanes through `.Resolve(vault)`, and exposed `GetStateAsRef` over `GetElementAsRef`. The bundle is reused by editor tooling and scheduled cognition jobs, so stale descriptors could corrupt apex state, signals, tuning, CSV scratch, or blackbox telemetry after Vault relocation.
Solution: Convert `ApexBrainVaultHandles` to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, acquire unlocked lanes through `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, borrow locked lanes through `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>`, and bind `ApexBrainVaultBuffers` through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`. Replace the byref accessor with value-copy `TryReadState` and `TryWriteState`. Add `ReleaseOwnedHandles` for the exact apex BufferIDs.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the public byref helper was rejected because refs can escape the phase-local view. Rewriting cognition scoring, signal queues, CSV parser, or dump format was rejected because those paths already consume local arrays; the defect was handle lifetime. Owner-wide release was rejected because `SystemID.AICognition` is shared by adjacent cognition vaults.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep 5 Hz quality-gated cognition cadence and emergency mock data. Middle/high/ultra retain richer acoustic taps, ambush nodes, signal queues, visual-overkill tuning, CSV hot reload, and 300-frame dumps through the same BufferIDs and DTO layouts.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation once per bundle view binding. The apex cognition Burst job still receives local arrays with `[NoAlias]` fields and does not validate descriptors per leviathan, acoustic tap, or influence node.

## Decision 105 - Trade marauder runtime removes pointer descriptors from economy lanes
Problem: `TradeMarauderRuntime.cs` persisted twenty-three `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors for marauder state, inventories, economy weights, sector data, A* scratch, telemetry, tuning, faction standing, mock inventory, signal scratch, loot, sector hash, CSV scratch, counters, route plans, and visual proxy lanes. It resolved those descriptors with `.Resolve(_vault)` from FrostTick, editor, CSV, reputation, and post-job publish paths, leaving economy proof/state lanes exposed to stale native addresses after Vault relocation.
Solution: Replace every trade marauder lane field with `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`. Cold setup now validates existing descriptors with `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>`/`TryResolveHandle`, allocates with `GetGenerationHandle<T>` only while the Vault is unlocked, and opens method-local `NativeArray<T>` views through `TryOpenVaultView`. Non-deferred teardown and DataVault rebind release exact owner descriptors through `ReleaseBuffer(in handle)`.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting A* pathfinding, offscreen theft, trade negotiation, acoustic ping publication, or visual proxy hydration was rejected because those jobs already consume local arrays and the defect was handle lifetime. Forcing `JobHandle.Complete()` during `OnDisable` was rejected because the dispatcher owns completion windows and hidden teardown stalls violate phase discipline. Owner-wide release was rejected because exact BufferID release is safer than broad owner release under parallel-agent edits.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` collapse of item evaluation, route solves, and A* iteration budget. Middle devices keep normal trade simulation with bounded signal scratch. High/ultra keep richer route planning, acoustic signatures, telemetry, and visual proxy payloads through the same DTO layouts and BufferIDs.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one generation validation per opened lane at setup/FrostTick/editor/CSV/publish boundaries. A* node loops, inventory loops, theft negotiation, visual hydration, acoustic signature, and telemetry jobs still operate on local `NativeArray<T>` values with no per-node descriptor validation or managed allocation.

## Decision 106 - Alpha Leviathan cognition vault removes pointer descriptors
Problem: `AlphaLeviathanCognitionVault.cs` persisted a public `AlphaLeviathanVaultHandles` bundle made of `VaultBufferHandle<T>` rows and resolved them through `.Resolve(vault)`. The bridge also exposed a raw `TryAcquireBuffers` path over `GetBuffer<T>`/`TryGetBuffer<T>`, allowing callers to cache native views without a current generation proof.
Solution: Convert the five Alpha cognition lanes to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` and bind views through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle` at method boundaries only. `TryAcquireBuffers` now acquires descriptors first and then resolves transient views, so even compatibility callers pass through the generation route. `ReleaseOwnedHandles` releases only the exact five owner descriptors.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting tangent-orbit stalking, SDF contour collapse, phase byte compatibility, or the blackbox binary dump was rejected because those systems already operate on local arrays and the defect was descriptor lifetime. Keeping direct `GetBuffer<T>` compatibility was rejected because it preserved a raw-view cache path. Owner-wide release was rejected because `SystemID.AICognition` is shared by apex cognition and other predator cognition lanes.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep survival radial fallback, 5 Hz-ish quality-pressure cadence, and cheap tangent-orbit steering. Middle devices keep normal fog-ring stalking and telemetry. High/ultra keep SDF contour weight, visual overkill shader scalars in the steering output, richer wake/silt/biolum payloads, and full 300-frame blackbox rows through identical DTO layouts.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays five descriptor validations at acquire/resolve boundaries. The Burst stalk job still receives local `NativeArray<T>` values and no per-slot descriptor check; `AlphaLeviathanCognitionState=192`, `AlphaLeviathanSensoryStimulus=176`, `AlphaLeviathanSteeringOutput=128`, and telemetry `=64` remain aligned and unchanged.

## Decision 107 - Data Archaeology removes pointer descriptors and binary scanner tier gate
Problem: `DataArchaeologyRuntime.cs` persisted `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors for discovery word, notification, and telemetry lanes, resolved them through `.Resolve(vault)`, and repeatedly polled `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` from private resolver paths. The same file also used `GlobalRegistry.ScalabilityTier`/`HectonQualityTier` to skip scanner shader points outright on low tiers.
Solution: Convert the three lanes to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, cache `IDataVault` only from cold lifecycle/hotswap code, and open method-local views through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`. Allocation/grow routes are explicitly named `TryOpenOrAcquire*` so read-style accessors do not hide mutation. Safe teardown/rebind releases the exact three owner descriptors. Scanner presentation now uses `HomeostasisBrain.GlobalQualityWeight` with a smoothstep progress scale.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping `TryResolve*` methods that allocate was rejected because read accessors must be pure by doctrine. Owner-wide release was rejected because `SystemID.GameplayTools` is shared. Low-tier scanner early return was rejected because binary quality gates create visible feature popping and violate continuous scalability.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep scanner feedback as a dim/cheap shader scalar rather than a hard-off feature. Middle devices get partial progress visibility. High/ultra retain full scanner shader progress and existing notification/telemetry fidelity through unchanged BufferIDs and DTO layouts.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at setup, lore sync, notification enqueue/dequeue, telemetry commit, and blackbox dump boundaries. Discovery bit loops and telemetry row writes still use local `NativeArray<T>` views with no per-row descriptor validation; removing the binary tier gate avoids branchy presentation policy tied to stale enum state.

## Decision 108 - Base atmosphere evicts cached native views and releases descriptors after job fences
Problem: `BaseAtmosphereEngine.cs` persisted four `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors and also cached four Vault-backed `NativeArray<T>` views as fields. Front/back compartment arrays are swapped after a cold tick job, so stale native views here could survive Vault relocation or DataVault replacement. The original teardown only cleared aliases and descriptors.
Solution: Store only `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` descriptors for front/back compartment lanes, CO2 byte lane, and blackbox telemetry. Resolve method-local native views at setup, seed, schedule, mutation, and blackbox boundaries. Swap front/back descriptors after the job completes. Public read routes use `IDataVault.TryReadHandle`; mutation/schedule routes use `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`. Exact owner release is immediate when no job is active and deferred through captured generation descriptors when the cold tick job fence is still pending.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping cached `NativeArray<T>` fields was rejected because they are raw address aliases even without explicit pointers. Forcing `JobHandle.Complete()` on teardown was rejected because dispatcher-owned completion windows must remain non-blocking. Owner-wide release was rejected because exact BufferID release is sufficient and safer under shared `SystemID.HabitatAtmosphere`.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing continuous cold-tick cadence, reduced solve budget, Dalton pressure fake, and byte CO2 scrub lane. Middle/high/ultra keep richer compartment counts, visual-overkill flags, and 300-frame telemetry through the same BufferIDs and DTO layouts.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at setup, schedule, seed, compartment mutation, and blackbox write boundaries. The cold tick job still receives local contiguous arrays and no per-compartment descriptor validation; deferred release avoids a teardown main-thread stall.

## Decision 109 - Surface weather output lane moves to generation descriptor
Problem: `HectonSurfaceWeatherDirector.cs` kept a `VaultBufferHandle<SurfaceWeatherJobOutput>`, resolved it with `.Resolve(vault)` for seed/schedule/complete, and pulled `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` from the output resolver. Disposal also used `forceComplete: true`, creating a hidden teardown stall.
Solution: Store `VaultGenerationHandle<SurfaceWeatherJobOutput>` only. Cache `IDataVault` during lifecycle setup, acquire the output lane through `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, and open method-local output views through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`. Disposal now attempts non-forced completion and releases the exact descriptor only when the fence permits it.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping hot `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` lookup in `TryResolveWeatherJobOutput` was rejected because the registry is cold DI. Keeping `forceComplete: true` was rejected because weather output disposal must not seize the dispatcher completion window. Rewriting the weather math job or AUP-safe origin changes was rejected because the unsafe route was the output lane descriptor.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep screen-space rain shedding and cheap scalar weather output. Middle/high/ultra retain richer storm/lightning/squall math through the same single output DTO and existing continuous weather quality decisions.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one descriptor validation at weather output seed/schedule/complete boundaries. The Burst job still writes a one-element `NativeSlice<SurfaceWeatherJobOutput>` with no per-parameter descriptor validation, and teardown no longer forces a main-thread wait.

## Decision 110 - Cable debug gizmo borrows generation descriptors only
