Problem: `CablePhysicsDebugGizmo132.cs` used `TryGetBufferHandle` and `.Resolve(vault)` to draw solver-owned cable nodes and constraints. Even though this is diagnostic code, it kept the obsolete pointer-bearing descriptor route alive.
Solution: Borrow `VaultGenerationHandle<CableNodeDTO>` and `VaultGenerationHandle<TetherConstraintDTO>` through `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>`, then open read-only diagnostic views through `IDataVault.TryReadHandle`.
Rejected Alternatives: Releasing the cable buffers from the gizmo was rejected because the cable solver owns those lanes. Adding per-node descriptor checks was rejected because the draw loop should consume local read views.
Scalability potential: Low devices can disable gizmos entirely through editor visibility; high/ultra debugging keeps full cable visualization without changing solver DTOs or runtime authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays two descriptor reads per gizmo draw. The node/constraint loops do not validate descriptors per element and no managed allocation was added.

## Decision 111 - Cable solver fallback surface removes pointer descriptors
Problem: `CablePhysicsSolver132.cs` still used `GetBufferHandle(...).Resolve(...)` for mock bootstrap lanes, mock view binding, telemetry sampling, and blackbox dump reads. `Shinobu132CablePhysicsTunerWindow.cs` also wrote tuning/material lanes through the same legacy pointer descriptor API.
Solution: Add generation-safe solver helpers that acquire `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` descriptors, open method-local `NativeArray<T>` views through `TryResolveHandle`, and use `TryReadHandle` for telemetry/dump reads. The editor tuner now calls those helpers for tuning and material CSV writes.
Rejected Alternatives: Cleaning only the editor window was rejected because the unsafe pointer route would remain in the runtime helper surface. Releasing buffers from editor code was rejected because the solver owns the cable lanes. Reworking TetherManager bootstrap scheduling was rejected in this pass because the defect was descriptor lifetime, not dispatcher cadence.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the continuous quality-weight mock tether collapse, lower iteration counts, and reduced spline vertices. Middle devices keep normal editor tuning and telemetry. High/ultra keep richer spline vertex counts, material CSV tuning, full telemetry dump, and visual-overkill cable motion through unchanged DTO layouts and BufferIDs.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays generation validation at bootstrap, schedule view binding, telemetry read, dump, and editor CSV/tuning boundaries. The cable jobs still operate on local `NativeArray<T>` and raw node pointers already bounded by job-local invariants, without per-node descriptor validation or managed allocation.

## Decision 112 - Macro ecosystem editor tuner removes byref Vault mutation
Problem: `MacroEcosystemTunerWindow.cs` used `VaultBufferHandle<MacroEcosystemTuningDTO>`, `TryGetBufferHandle`, `.Resolve(vault)`, `GetElementAsRef`, and `TryGetBuffer<T>` for graph telemetry. The editor byref write could outlive the descriptor validation boundary and kept a raw-buffer read path alive.
Solution: Replace tuning reads with `TryReadHandle`, tuning writes with `TryResolveHandle` plus copy-modify-write through `buffer[0]`, and telemetry graph reads with `TryReadHandle` from a borrowed `VaultGenerationHandle<MacroEcosystemTelemetryEntry>`.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the byref write was rejected because ref escape is precisely the stale pointer class being removed. Allocating or releasing macro ecosystem lanes from the editor was rejected because the runtime ecosystem owner owns those buffers.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same macro ecology runtime quality/cadence behavior; this editor facade only tunes existing coefficients and draws existing telemetry. High/ultra retain full telemetry graph density without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, or simulation authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is zero because the window is editor-only. Editor repaint pays one descriptor read for telemetry, and each slider mutation pays one descriptor write-open, with no per-row descriptor validation inside graph drawing.

## Decision 113 - Voxel save tuner uses generation descriptors for tuning and telemetry
Problem: `VoxelSaveTunerWindow.cs` used three `GetBufferHandle(...).Resolve(...)` tuning routes plus a generic `TryGetBufferHandle`/`.Resolve` helper for telemetry, cursor, sector stats, histogram, and SceneView heatmap data.
Solution: Add a save-tuning generation helper that reuses an existing `VaultGenerationHandle<VoxelDeltaCompressionTuningDTO>` or allocates through `GetGenerationHandle<T>` only when allocations are not locked. Replace visualization reads with borrowed generation descriptors and `TryReadHandle`.
Rejected Alternatives: Leaving editor visualization on `TryGetBuffer<T>`/legacy handles was rejected because editor code can still survive Vault relocation and normalize unsafe API usage. Releasing save buffers from the tuner was rejected because SavePersistence owns those lanes.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same WAL cadence and byte budget values authored by the tuner. Middle/high/ultra retain higher write cadence and richer histogram/heatmap diagnostics without changing tuning DTO layout or save identity.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is zero because the file is editor-only. Editor summary, histogram, and heatmap pay one generation descriptor read per repaint/pass; no descriptor validation is introduced inside the sector or telemetry drawing loops.

## Decision 114 - Seed ship anomaly editor tuner preserves locks while removing pointer handles
Problem: `SeedShipAnomalyTunerWindow.cs` used `TryGetBufferHandle` and `.Resolve(vault)` for anomaly field, tuning, and global scalar editor reads and writes.
Solution: Read field, tuning, and globals through borrowed `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` descriptors with `TryReadHandle`. For writes, borrow field/tuning descriptors first, keep the existing `TryLockBuffer`/`TryUnlockBuffer` ownership window, and resolve mutable views with `TryResolveHandle` inside that window.
Rejected Alternatives: Removing the existing anomaly locks was rejected because editor writes still mutate runtime-owned EndgameAnomaly lanes. Releasing or allocating anomaly buffers from the editor was rejected because the runtime anomaly system owns those lanes.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same shader/global scalar anomaly presentation and corruption radius tuning. High/ultra retain full editor gizmo radius visualization and tuning precision without changing DTO layouts, BufferIDs, or anomaly authority.

## Decision 115 - Submarine dyno tuner removes latest-vault and legacy handle reads
Problem: `SubmarineDynoTunerWindow.cs` used `GlobalDataVault.TryGetLatestCreated`, `TryGetBufferHandle`, `GetElementAsReadOnlyRef`, and `.Resolve(vault)` to read and mutate submarine kinematic/config/force buffers from an editor window.
Solution: Route editor snapshots through cold `GlobalRegistry.DataVault`, borrowed `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` descriptors, and `IDataVault.TryReadHandle`. Route config writes through the existing generation descriptor plus a short `SystemID.CoreDiagnostics` writer fence with guaranteed release.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping `TryGetLatestCreated` was rejected because it is documented as bootstrap/editor diagnostic only and still bypasses the registry-owned dependency route. Keeping direct `.Resolve(vault)` mutation was rejected because the write had no explicit writer fence. Releasing or allocating submarine buffers from the editor was rejected because `VehiclesPhysics` owns those lanes.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same submarine dynamics truth and editor-only refresh cadence. Middle/high/ultra retain full dyno tuning and SceneView vector visualization without changing DTO layouts, BufferIDs, job cadence, rollback identity, or quality-weight semantics.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is zero because the window is editor-only. Editor refresh pays three descriptor reads; slider edits pay one descriptor open plus one writer-fence transition, with no descriptor validation inside handle drawing.

## Decision 116 - Verlet tow editor tuner stops using latest-vault pointer handles
Problem: `VerletTowTunerWindow.cs` used `GlobalDataVault.TryGetLatestCreated`, `GetBufferHandle`, `TryGetBufferHandle`, and `.Resolve(vault)` for tuning, material CSV reload, and SceneView tension visualization.

Tuning/material rows open through `TryResolveHandle` and only allocate with `GetGenerationHandle<T>` when allocations are not locked; visual positions/tensions read through `TryReadHandle`.

Releasing tether visual lanes from the editor was rejected because physics owns them. Adding a runtime job or new telemetry buffer was rejected because the existing gizmo is a visual diagnostic facade.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep cheap editor gizmo drawing and existing cable math-LOD values. Middle/high/ultra retain CSV material tuning and full 8-slot, 10-segment tension visualization without changing cable DTO layouts, BufferIDs, or physics authority.

Adding new somatic runtime telemetry or jobs was rejected because the existing blackbox/comfort telemetry lanes already prove the facade without changing gameplay ownership.

Problem: `VolumetricSiltTunerWindow.cs` used `GetBuffer<T>` for VFX tuning mutation and `TryGetBuffer<T>` for dynamic wake gizmo reads. The first migration draft also assumed the strict 16-byte `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` carried length/creation fields, which would violate the handle ABI.
Solution: Read tuning and wakes through borrowed generation descriptors with `TryReadHandle`. Seed and write tuning through `TryAcquireWriteLock` under `SystemID.CoreDiagnostics`, releasing in `finally`. Rejected Alternatives: Keeping direct `GetBuffer<T>` was rejected because editor UI can outlive relocation. Expanding `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` with length was rejected because Task 04 requires a rigid 16-byte ARM64-friendly descriptor. Adding runtime wake telemetry was rejected because the existing editor gizmo is a visual facade over VFX-owned lanes.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same cheap capped 16-wake wire-disc visualization and VFX tuning facade. Middle/high/ultra retain full marine snow tuning, CSV-owned renderer behavior, and wake visualization without changing DTO layouts, BufferIDs, shader payloads, or VFX authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is zero because the file is editor-only. Editor repaint pays one descriptor read for tuning; edits pay one writer-fence transition; SceneView pays one descriptor read before a capped gizmo loop.

Problem: `EcologySymbiosisTunerWindow.cs` used `TryGetBufferHandle`, `GetElementAsReadOnlyRef`, `GetElementAsRef`, and `.Resolve(vault)` for tuning, counters, and six SceneView gizmo buffers.

Releasing ecology-owned lanes from the editor was rejected because `SystemID.AIEcology` owns symbiosis buffers. Problem: `AbyssalSwarmTunerWindow.cs` used `VaultBufferHandle<ShinobuEcosystemTuning>` with `GetElementAsRef` for tuning mutation and direct `TryGetBuffer<T>` views for bridge status, counters, telemetry, spatial hash cells, ambient entities, and ambient AUPs. Middle/high/ultra retain full tuning, species profile visibility, spatial hash debugging, and boid vector diagnostics without changing DTO layouts, BufferIDs, GlobalQualityWeight behavior, or AIEcology authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is zero because the file is editor-only. Editor UI/SceneView pays descriptor reads per pass; the species scan, graph bars, hash cells, and vector loops remain descriptor-free after the local view opens.

## Decision 122 - SHINOBU 143 cable tuner removes latest-vault pointer route
Problem: `Shinobu143CablePhysicsTunerWindow.cs` used `GlobalDataVault.TryGetLatestCreated`, `GetBufferHandle<T>`, and `.Resolve(vault)` to open tuning and material lanes. The window duplicated cable editor debt already removed from the SHINOBU 132 tuner and kept a legacy pointer route for `VerletCableTuning`, `VerletCableMaterials`, and `Shinobu143CableMaterials`.
Solution: Use cold `GlobalRegistry.DataVault`, acquire/grow lanes through `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` only when allocation is not locked, and mutate them under `SystemID.CoreDiagnostics` writer fences with guaranteed release. Keep telemetry and dump reads delegated to existing `TetherAupRuntimeIntrospection` bridges instead of adding new editor state.
Rejected Alternatives: Reusing `TryGetLatestCreated` was rejected because the registry is the cold DI route for editor facades. Returning a write-locked `NativeArray<T>` from a helper without the handle was rejected because release ownership would be implicit. Adding new cable telemetry buffers was rejected because telemetry already exists and the defect was stale descriptor access.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep cheap editor-only tuning and 0.25s telemetry refresh. Middle/high/ultra retain CSV material authoring, tuning precision, and cable surgeon dumps without changing DTO layouts, BufferIDs, cable solver cadence, or physics authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is zero because the file is editor-only. CSV reload pays two writer-fence transitions before parsing and performs no descriptor validation inside the material parse loop.

## Decision 123 - Abyssal atmosphere tuner separates read and write Vault intent
Problem: `AbyssalAtmosphereTunerWindow.cs` used mutable `TryResolveHandle` for fog param refresh/slider edits, direct `GetBuffer<T>` for extinction profile and CSV scratch writes, and mutable telemetry resolve for a read-only graph. The file had generation descriptors in places but not explicit write/read authority.
Solution: Route fog param refresh/default seeding and slider writes through `TryAcquireWriteLock` under `SystemID.CoreDiagnostics`. Route CSV scratch and extinction profile lanes through the same writer-fence helper. Route the telemetry graph through borrowed descriptors and `TryReadHandle`.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping `TryResolveHandle` for editor convenience was rejected because the code mutates defaults during refresh and mutates slider values during callbacks. Holding the profile writer lock during file IO was rejected; scratch is written first, then profile lock is taken for parse/upsert. Adding a new VFX telemetry buffer was rejected because the graph already consumes the runtime telemetry ring.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep cheap editor graph and continuous fog quality controls. Middle/high/ultra retain extinction CSV authoring, visual-overkill quality sliders, ray-step tuning, and telemetry visualization without changing DTO layouts, shader payloads, BufferIDs, or VFX authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is zero because the file is editor-only. CSV load validates scratch/profile descriptors once and parses without descriptor checks; graph repaint validates telemetry once before the sample loop.

## Decision 124 - AUP scanner telemetry histogram stops using direct buffer reads
Problem: `AUP_Premature_Cast_Scanner.cs` still read `AupPrecisionVault.TelemetryRingBuffer` through direct `TryGetBuffer<T>` for the histogram.
Solution: Borrow a `VaultGenerationHandle<AupPrecisionTelemetryEntry>` and read the telemetry ring through `IDataVault.TryReadHandle`.
Rejected Alternatives: Leaving the diagnostic path on `TryGetBuffer<T>` was rejected because the SHINOBU_202 cleanup is consumer-wide and editor diagnostics are long-lived enough to survive Vault relocation. Reworking the scanner jobs or TempJob fallback was rejected because the direct Vault read was the only defect in this pass.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same optional telemetry histogram and TempJob fallback. Middle/high/ultra retain the full AUP precision histogram without changing DTO layout, scan output, or math authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is zero because the file is editor-only. Histogram refresh pays one descriptor read before passing the local ring view to the existing sampler.

## Decision 125 - Construction socket editor summary and gizmo borrow read descriptors
Problem: `ConstructionSocketEditorTools.cs` read construction counters, telemetry, socket states, and socket AUPs through direct `TryGetBuffer<T>` in editor summary and gizmo paths.
Solution: Add a shared editor-only read helper that borrows `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` descriptors and opens read views through `IDataVault.TryReadHandle`.
Rejected Alternatives: Leaving editor gizmos on `TryGetBuffer<T>` was rejected because SceneView tools are long-lived diagnostics and can survive Vault relocation. Adding writer locks was rejected because the paths are read-only summary/gizmo consumers.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep cheap socket counts and bounded gizmo drawing. Middle/high/ultra retain construction socket visualization without changing DTO layout, tuning ownership, candidate budgets, or construction authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is zero because the file is editor-only. Summary and gizmo passes pay descriptor reads once before existing loops and formatting.

## Decision 126 - Grid and genome editors stop using mutable resolve for read paths
Problem: `GridArchitectTunerWindow.cs` used `TryResolveHandle` for power telemetry ring/cursor reads, and `LSystemGenomeLabWindow.cs` used `TryResolveHandle` across a mixed read/edit/preview GUI path.
Solution: Replace grid telemetry opens with `TryReadHandle`. Replace L-system genome GUI read with `TryReadHandle` and reacquire a short `SystemID.CoreDiagnostics` writer fence only when a genome row changes.
Rejected Alternatives: Leaving cached telemetry handles on mutable resolve was rejected because the telemetry path is read-only. Holding a genome writer lock across preview/job execution was rejected because the preview is read-only and can run nontrivial editor work.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep cheap editor telemetry and mock genome preview. Middle/high/ultra retain full grid power telemetry and editable L-system genome preview without changing DTO layouts, job inputs, or owner authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is zero because both files are editor-only. Grid refresh pays two descriptor reads; genome GUI pays one read plus one writer-fence transition only on actual edits.

## Decision 127 - Verlet tow and builder holography editor writes require explicit fences
Problem: `VerletTowTunerWindow.cs` still used `TryResolveHandle` to mutate cable tuning/material lanes, and `BuilderHolographyTools.cs` used a latest-created Vault fallback, a runtime all-view resolver for UI reads, and a raw native pointer/ref in the profile CSV parser.
Solution: Move Verlet tuning/material writes to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` descriptors plus `SystemID.CoreDiagnostics` writer fences released in `finally`. Move Builder Holography UI reads to lane-specific `TryReadHandle` calls, route slider writes through a short writer fence, and parse CSV into a local DTO copy before writing row `0`.
Rejected Alternatives: Treating editor windows as safe exceptions was rejected because they are long-lived and survive Vault relocation. Keeping `TryResolveVaultViews` for the tuner was rejected because it returns broad mutable runtime views when the UI needs only tuning and telemetry lanes. Keeping the raw parser ref was rejected because direct pointer extraction is precisely the stale-alias pattern SHINOBU_202 is removing.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep cheap editor refreshes and existing construction/cable runtime quality behavior. Middle/high/ultra retain full tuning, CSV authoring, telemetry histograms, and construction holography diagnostics without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, solver cadence, construction authority, or continuous `GlobalQualityWeight` semantics.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is zero because both files are editor-only. Editor tuning reads validate descriptors once per refresh, edits pay one writer-fence transition, and CSV parsing stays descriptor-free after the local view opens.

## Decision 128 - Vault X-Ray uses the registry-published IDataVault route
Problem: `VaultXRayWindow.cs` used `GlobalDataVault.TryGetLatestCreated` for snapshot refresh, force defrag, and CSV override reload. That was allowed only for narrow bootstrap/editor diagnostic exceptions, but this window can use the registered dependency without losing any X-Ray surface.
Solution: Replace the static latest-created lookups with `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` and call the existing `IDataVault` telemetry, memory map, generation id, defrag request, and CSV override APIs through that interface.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping latest-created because the window is editor-only was rejected because it normalizes a fallback route when the registry-published owner is available. Casting to concrete `GlobalDataVault` was rejected because `IDataVault` already exposes the required diagnostic methods.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same 0.5s editor refresh and quality-scaled stride readout. Middle/high/ultra retain full waterfall and block-map diagnostics without changing Vault DTO layout, telemetry ring identity, defrag authority, or memory budget CSV semantics.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is zero because the file is editor-only. Editor refresh cost is unchanged: one registry read followed by the existing bounded 64-sample waterfall and 256-block memory-map pass.

## Decision 129 - Ocean surface atmosphere stores strict generation descriptors
Problem: `ShinobuOceanSurfaceAtmosphereRuntime.cs` persisted thirteen legacy `VaultBufferHandle<T>` fields, used direct `TryGetBuffer<T>` for tuner/debug snapshots, kept a latest-created diagnostic fallback, and applied tuner values directly into runtime-owned lanes without writer fences.
Solution: Replace all persisted ocean Vault handles with 16-byte `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` descriptors. Resolve every lane through `_vault.TryResolveHandle` at method scope, read editor/debug snapshots through `TryReadHandle`, remove latest-created fallback, and wrap static tuner writes in `SystemID.CoreDiagnostics` writer fences released in `finally`.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping legacy handles because `.Resolve(vault)` refreshes pointers was rejected because the handle itself still carries cached pointer metadata. Keeping direct `TryGetBuffer<T>` for editor snapshots was rejected because snapshot APIs are long-lived UI consumers and should not normalize direct buffer access. Adding new runtime allocations or buffer IDs was rejected because the existing lanes already own wave/atmosphere truth.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep continuous wave evaluation throttling, cheap readback cadence, and existing shader globals. Middle/high/ultra retain full wave payload upload, Beaufort profile tuning, GPU readback diagnostics, and visual-overkill ocean shader parameters without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or authority route.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is a single generation validation per lane open, not per wave lane. Editor slider writes pay bounded writer-fence transitions; frame-loop wave math, telemetry serialization, readback ring traversal, and GPU upload loops remain descriptor-free after local views open.

## Decision 130 - Submarine OS thermal grid stops exporting Vault pointers
Problem: `SubmarineOsThermalGridRuntime.cs` persisted twenty legacy `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors, used `GetBufferHandle<T>` and `handle.Resolve(vault)`, kept an editor latest-created fallback, and exported `SubmarineThermalGridTuningDTO*` to two editor windows. That allowed a long-lived editor pointer to outlive Vault relocation and kept stale pointer metadata in a runtime Power solver.
Solution: Replace persisted thermal-grid handles with 16-byte `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` descriptors. Resolve method-local `NativeArray<T>` views through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`. Remove the latest-created fallback from the runtime route. Replace the raw tuning pointer API with `TryReadTuning` and `TryApplyTuning`; editor windows now edit DTO copies and commit through `SystemID.CoreDiagnostics` writer fences. CSV reload opens CSV/spec/tuning/counter lanes through writer-fence views and releases them in `finally`.
Rejected Alternatives: Leaving the pointer API as editor-only was rejected because editor windows survive play-mode reloads and Vault relocation. Converting the solver jobs away from raw pointers in this pass was rejected because the jobs already receive pointers only after method-local descriptor resolution and that broader Burst-signature rewrite would expand the blast radius. Using `GlobalDataVault.TryGetLatestCreated` as an editor fallback inside the runtime was rejected because the runtime already has a registry/injected Vault route and a standalone editor Vault fallback for isolated windows.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same continuous solver-quality curve, residual sample mask, shader scalar publishing, and emergency mock topology. Middle/high/ultra retain full Jacobi iteration scaling, telemetry, CSV tuning, and visual-overkill shader scalar behavior without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or Power authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is one generation validation per lane open before schedule/readback/commit. Hot Jacobi jobs, topology copy, telemetry write, and shader scalar loops remain descriptor-free after local view binding. Editor slider writes pay one tuning writer-fence transition; CSV reload pays four writer-fence transitions before allocation-free byte parsing.

## Decision 131 - RTG decay lanes use generation descriptors
Problem: `RadioisotopeThermalGenerator.cs` persisted seven static `VaultBufferHandle<T>` lanes and opened them through `GetBufferHandle<T>` plus `handle.Resolve(vault)`. The helper also polled `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` on every open and relied on handle length/created properties that do not exist on the strict 16-byte descriptor.
Solution: Replace the seven static lanes with `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` descriptors, add a cached `IDataVault` reference reset during subsystem registration, and rewrite the shared resolver to use `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, `TryGetGenerationHandle`, and `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`. The fast path returns after a single successful descriptor resolution; allocation/reborrow is only used when the descriptor is missing, stale, too small, or the Vault allocation state requires borrowing.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the static legacy handle wrapper was rejected because decay jobs and blackbox serialization can outlive relocation windows. Polling `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` per resolver call was rejected because the registry is a cold dependency route. Adding writer locks around the existing single-owner RTG mutation path was rejected in this pass because the RTG leader already serializes decay completion and no cross-system writer was introduced.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing low-cadence decay path and cheap RTG warning behavior. Middle/high/ultra retain full 300-entry telemetry, save integration, thermodynamics/radiation signals, and visual/audio warning surfaces without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, or Power authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is one generation validation per lane open before scheduled decay slices or telemetry reads. The decay job still works over `NativeSlice<T>` views after local binding, so the 128-slot decay loop has no per-element descriptor validation.

## Decision 132 - Logistics router lanes use strict generation descriptors
Problem: `ShinobuLogisticsRouter.cs` persisted twenty-five `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors and opened them through `GetBufferHandle<T>`, `TryGetBufferHandle<T>`, and `handle.Resolve(vault)`. The router feeds scheduled Burst jobs for CSR rebuild, flow propagation, telemetry, and local AUP shifts, so stale pointer-bearing handles are a relocation/UAF risk.
Solution: Replace every persistent logistics handle with `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`. Rewrite `ResolveVaultBuffer` and `RefreshVaultBuffer` to use `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>`, and `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`. Add an explicit descriptor validity test on `BufferID`. Route public `SetTuning` writes through a `SystemID.Power` writer fence with release in `finally`.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping legacy handles because `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle(in VaultBufferHandle<T>)` ignores cached pointers was rejected because the persistent wrapper still normalizes pointer-era metadata. Rewriting all runtime `NativeArray<T>` cached aliases in this pass was rejected because the bounded defect is stale descriptor storage and the aliases are rebound at phase entry. Adding per-element descriptor checks inside solver jobs was rejected because it would destroy SIMD-friendly hot loops.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing continuous quality curve: fewer Jacobi iterations, lower oxygen cadence, emergency mock topology, and bounded telemetry. Middle/high/ultra retain higher propagation fidelity, richer flow visualization, CSR flow scalars, and blackbox telemetry without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or Power authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is one generation validation per lane open before phase binding. Hot BFS, Jacobi pressure solve, finalization, telemetry hashing, CSR rebuild, and local AUP shift loops remain descriptor-free after local view binding.

## Decision 133 - Battery charger borrows external inventory through descriptors
Problem: `BatteryChargerLogisticsRuntime.cs` already stored its owned lanes as `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, but it still accessed the external `BufferID.ShinobuInventorySlots` lane through direct `TryGetBuffer` calls in link registration, slot writes, charge reads, and simulation binding. That bypassed the strict generation descriptor route for a cross-domain inventory fact.
Solution: Add method-local helpers that borrow `VaultGenerationHandle<InventorySlotDTO>` through `TryGetGenerationHandle<InventorySlotDTO>`, then open the live inventory lane through `TryReadHandle`, `TryResolveHandle`, or `TryAcquireWriteLock` depending on access intent. Convert `TryWriteInventorySlotState` to release the descriptor writer fence in `finally` while preserving its row-level `ReservedLock` guard.
Rejected Alternatives: Caching a persistent borrowed inventory descriptor was rejected because the lane is owned by the Inventory authority and the battery charger only needs phase-local access. Leaving `TryLockBuffer` plus direct `TryGetBuffer` was rejected because the BufferID lock does not prove descriptor freshness. Rewriting the entire battery charger lock mask system was rejected because owned charger lanes already use descriptors and the remaining defect was the external inventory bridge.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing cadence curve and mock inventory fallback. Middle/high/ultra retain live charger link simulation, shader upload, telemetry, CSV profiles, and richer visual state without changing inventory DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or Power/Inventory authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is one generation validation when binding the external live inventory lane. The scheduled charger job still receives a local pointer after phase binding, so per-link charge updates remain descriptor-free.

## Decision 134 - Procedural wreckage facade drops pointer-bearing handles
Problem: `ProceduralWreckageVault.cs` exposed `VaultBufferHandle<T>` in its public handle facade, allocated with `GetBufferHandle<T>`, borrowed with `TryGetBufferHandle<T>`, and resolved every lane through `handles.X.Resolve(vault)`. That made every procedural wreckage consumer carry stale pointer metadata across frame/facade boundaries.
Solution: Convert the facade to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` for all eighteen lanes. Use `GetGenerationHandle<T>` and `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>` for allocation/borrow. Replace every view bind with `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle` in a single method-local helper. Keep the public `IsCreated()` shape for callers, but implement it as non-zero `BufferID` checks.
Rejected Alternatives: Expanding `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` with length/created state was rejected because SHINOBU_202 requires a 16-byte descriptor. Rewriting downstream wreckage jobs was rejected because they already consume local `NativeArray<T>` views, use deterministic Burst flags, and carry `[NoAlias]`; the unsafe retention defect was the facade storage and resolver.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain cheap rule hydration, bounded render matrix capacity, HZB culling, and mock sector triggers. Middle/high/ultra retain full wreckage WFC collapse, debris extraction, indirect draw argument writes, loot requests, and GPU scalar richness without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or WorldStreaming authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is one generation validation per lane at facade bind. Collapse, shear, debris, loot, collision proxy, HZB cull, indirect draw argument, telemetry, and self-audit jobs remain descriptor-free after binding.

## Decision 135 - Procedural coral facade drops pointer-bearing handles
Problem: `ProceduralCoralVault.cs` exposed `VaultBufferHandle<T>` in its public handle facade, allocated with `GetBufferHandle<T>`, borrowed with `TryGetBufferHandle<T>`, and resolved twenty lanes through `handles.X.Resolve(vault)`. Coral generation schedules L-system expansion, spatial constraints, render extraction, HZB-aware matrix output, collision staging, telemetry, and self-audit jobs from these views, so facade-level stale pointer metadata is a relocation/UAF risk.
Solution: Convert the facade to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` for all twenty lanes. Use `GetGenerationHandle<T>` and `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>` for allocation/borrow. Replace every view bind with `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle` in a method-local helper and preserve the public `IsCreated()` shape as non-zero `BufferID` validation only.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the legacy facade because downstream jobs are already correct was rejected; correct job inputs do not justify stale pointer-bearing public handles. Rewriting downstream coral jobs was rejected because they already consume local `NativeArray<T>` views, use deterministic Burst flags, and carry `[NoAlias]`; the bounded defect was facade storage and resolution.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain cheap mock sector triggers, bounded branch counts, HZB tile culling, and shader sway payloads. Middle/high/ultra retain full L-system growth, branch constraints, bioluminescence injection, indirect draw arguments, collision proxy staging, sync pulses, and blackbox telemetry without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or WorldStreaming authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is one generation validation per lane at facade bind. L-system expansion, branch constraint, matrix extraction, bioluminescence, collision proxy, telemetry, and self-audit jobs remain descriptor-free after binding.

## Decision 136 - Voxel surface nets facade and byref helpers use generation descriptors
Problem: `VoxelSurfaceNetsVault.cs` exposed `VaultBufferHandle<T>` in its public handle facade, allocated with `GetBufferHandle<T>`, borrowed with `TryGetBufferHandle<T>`, resolved eighteen lanes through `handles.X.Resolve(vault)`, and retained `GetElementAsRef` / `GetElementAsReadOnlyRef` helpers for chunk meshing state. The byref helpers were an explicit stale pointer bridge.
Solution: Convert the facade to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` for all eighteen lanes. Use `GetGenerationHandle<T>` and `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>` for allocation/borrow. Replace view binding with `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`. Replace the state byref helpers with method-local state view resolution, explicit bounds checks, and `UnsafeUtility.ArrayElementAsRef` derived from the resolved local view.
Rejected Alternatives: Removing the byref helpers outright was rejected because callers may depend on the API shape. Keeping handle `GetElementAsRef` was rejected because it hides pointer resolution behind a legacy handle method. Rewriting the meshing jobs was rejected because they already consume local arrays, carry Burst compile directives, and use `[NoAlias]`; the defect was facade storage and byref access.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain cheap mock density, lower chunks-per-frame, decimation, HZB culling, and bounded raw-debug output. Middle/high/ultra retain full surface-net extraction, priority scheduling, GPU upload, physics-bake request emission, indirect draw arguments, and blackbox telemetry without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or WorldStreaming authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is one generation validation per lane at facade bind plus one validation for explicit state byref accessor calls. Density generation, surface extraction, priority, dirty signal, AABB shift, physics bake, HZB cull, GPU upload, telemetry, and CSV parsing remain descriptor-free after binding.

## Decision 137 - Vehicle damage runtime and contract open local Vault views before pointer use
Problem: `VehicleComponentDamageRuntime.cs` persisted eleven `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors, allocated through `GetBufferHandle<T>`, borrowed kinematic config through `TryGetBufferHandle<T>`, and used `.Resolve`, `.ResolvePointer`, `GetElementAsRef`, `GetElementAsReadOnlyRef`, and `ResolveBuffer` in fixed tick, initialization, CSV reload, editor snapshots, gizmos, root-pose reads, and blackbox dump paths. `VehicleComponentDamageContracts.cs` also exposed `VehicleDamageAccess.GetCellRef(ref VaultBufferHandle<VehicleGridCellDTO>)`.
Solution: Replace every persisted vehicle damage handle with `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`. Allocate/borrow through generation descriptors. Add local helpers that open `NativeArray<T>` views through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`, derive raw job pointers from those method-local views, and derive editor/readback refs with `UnsafeUtility.ArrayElementAsRef` only after descriptor validation. Change `VehicleDamageAccess.GetCellRef` to accept `in VaultGenerationHandle<VehicleGridCellDTO>` and resolve a local view before returning the ref.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting the pointer-based Burst jobs was rejected because the job fields already carry deterministic Burst flags and `[NoAlias]`; their inputs are safe when derived immediately before scheduling. Keeping the legacy byref helpers for editor/contract convenience was rejected because editor windows, gizmos, and contract helpers are long-lived enough to survive Vault relocation. Converting every existing `TryLockBuffer` call to the newer descriptor writer-fence API was deferred because this loop targeted stale handle storage and hidden pointer resolution without expanding the lock lifecycle surface.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep continuous mock signal count scaling, bounded gizmo cells, emergency mock damage, and existing damage-grid cadence. Middle/high/ultra retain full grid damage mapping, system scalar outputs, telemetry, CSV layout authoring, and blackbox dumps without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or VehiclesPhysics authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is one generation validation per lane before fixed tick jobs, CSV reload, editor readbacks, or gizmos. Damage mapping, reduction, state evaluation, publish, and telemetry jobs remain descriptor-free after binding.

## Decision 138 - Abyssal thermodynamics opens local descriptor views before solver pointers
Problem: `AbyssalThermodynamicsSolver.cs` persisted sixteen `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors and used `ResolvePointer`, `.Resolve`, handle `.IsCreated`, and `GetBufferHandle<T>` across tick scheduling, source mutation, sample jobs, boot initialization, CSV profile load, telemetry reads, GPU upload, blackbox dump, and editor gizmos.
Solution: Replace all persisted solver lanes with `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`. Allocate through `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, validate descriptors with `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`, and derive raw job pointers only from method-local `NativeArray<T>` views. Use `TryReadHandle` for pure read accessors and diagnostic readback. Route editor-facing tuning mutation through a `SystemID.CoreDiagnostics` writer fence released in `finally`.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting the Burst job pointer ABI was rejected because the job fields already carry deterministic Burst flags and `[NoAlias]`; the unsafe defect was stale handle storage and hidden pointer resolution, not the scheduled pointer inputs. Keeping direct editor tuning mutation was rejected because the UI Toolkit tuner can outlive Vault relocation. Adding new buffer IDs or DTO layouts was rejected because the existing lanes already own thermodynamics truth.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep continuous quality-driven cadence, lower stable grid resolution, fewer Jacobi passes, mock vent fallback, and cheap shader upload. Middle/high/ultra retain high-resolution heat diffusion, richer residual telemetry, blackbox dumps, source profiles, and GPU thermal visualization without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or Thermodynamics authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is one generation validation per lane before tick scheduling, source mutation, sampling, CSV load, upload, or dump. Jacobi diffusion, source injection, residual reduction, telemetry, and sample jobs remain descriptor-free after local binding.

## Decision 139 - Thermodynamics hazard grid removes stale handles and worker-retained Vault pointers
Problem: `ThermodynamicsHazardGridRuntime.cs` persisted nineteen `VaultBufferHandle<T>` lanes, resolved them through legacy handle APIs, exposed a raw constants pointer to `ThermodynamicsTunerWindow`, and let `ThermodynamicsHazardGridRuntime.FileWorker.cs` retain Vault byte pointers on a background thread. That combination can survive Vault relocation and makes editor/readback accessors mutate state through hidden routes.
Solution: Replace every persisted hazard lane with `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, allocate through `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, and open method-local `NativeArray<T>` views through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle` or `TryReadHandle`. Replace the editor constants pointer with DTO copy reads plus `SystemID.CoreDiagnostics` writer-fenced writes. Move mirror-grid mutation into explicit `PrepareVaultGridReadback()` and keep `TryGetVaultGridReadback(...)` read-only. Convert the config worker to cold byte staging arrays; pin only during file reads, then copy into Vault byte lanes on the main owner phase under `SystemID.Thermodynamics` writer fences before parsing constants.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the background thread on Vault pointers was rejected because MMF/disk IO can overlap relocation windows. Moving all config IO to the main thread was rejected because it would trade pointer safety for visible frame hitches. Keeping the raw editor constants pointer was rejected because editor windows are long-lived consumers and can outlive Vault relocation. Rewriting the deterministic Burst job pointer ABI was rejected because the unsafe retention defect was descriptor storage and hidden resolution, not the scheduled job inputs derived immediately before schedule.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain continuous lower-resolution hazard grids, lower update pressure, editor-only CSV suppression in player builds, shader heat texture upload, and emergency mock hazards. Middle/high/ultra retain high-resolution diffusion, explicit Vault mirror visualization, updraft signals, telemetry, CSV/binary tuning, and GPU thermal presentation without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or Thermodynamics authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is one generation validation per lane before phase binding and a bounded 16-byte/4096-byte config copy on rare reloads. Emission, diffusion, rebase, telemetry scan, signal publish, shader upload, and blackbox loops remain free of per-element descriptor validation; the file worker no longer retains raw Vault pointers across frames.

## Decision 140 - Fabrication assembler opens local descriptor views and keeps reads pure
Problem: `FabricationAssemblerRuntime.cs` persisted eight `VaultBufferHandle<T>` descriptors, allocated through `GetBufferHandle<T>`, resolved all lanes through `handle.Resolve(vault)`, and let `TryReadSnapshot`, editor stats/debug, and tuning reads call `EnsureVaultState()`. That meant read accessors could allocate/grow Vault buffers and stale pointer-bearing descriptors could survive relocation before fabrication progress jobs, signal emission, CSV import, telemetry, and GPU upload paths.
Solution: Replace every persisted fabrication lane with `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`. Owned lanes allocate through `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, borrowed scalability state uses `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>`, and all lane views open through method-local `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle` or `TryReadHandle` helpers. Read accessors now require the runtime to already be initialized and only use read-handle views. Tuning writes use a `SystemID.CoreDiagnostics` writer fence; CSV ingestion opens scratch, timing lookup, and tuning version lanes under bounded writer fences released in `finally`.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping legacy handles because the bridge ignores cached pointers was rejected because the runtime would still normalize pointer-era descriptor storage. Keeping read accessors on `EnsureVaultState()` was rejected because read APIs must not allocate, publish, or mutate state. Rewriting the existing Burst job structs was rejected because they already consume local `NativeArray<T>` views, carry deterministic Burst flags, and mark non-overlapping arrays with `[NoAlias]`; the unsafe defect was persistent descriptor storage and hidden resolution.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing continuous quality curve: lower GPU upload count, longer upload stride, low-tier signal capacity, and mock fabrication fallback. Middle/high/ultra retain full shader payload upload, richer fabrication tick/completion signals, CSV timing authoring, telemetry, and visual-overkill assembly edge glow without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or Construction authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 runtime cost is one generation validation per lane before public mutation, dispatcher job binding, telemetry write, GPU upload, or readback. Fabrication progress, signal emission, mock generation, telemetry scan, and shader upload loops remain descriptor-free after local view binding. Designer tuning/CSV writes pay bounded writer-fence atomics and no managed hot-path allocation.

## Decision 141 - Retinal adaptation facade resolves through generation descriptors
Problem: `RetinalAdaptationVault.cs` still opened `PredatorRetinalExposure`, blindness state, last-published state, light sources, and telemetry ring lanes through direct `IDataVault.GetBuffer<T>`. The file did not persist handles, but it normalized a direct mutable buffer route instead of the strict pointer-free descriptor route required by SHINOBU_202.
Solution: Replace each direct buffer allocation with a method-local `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` from `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, validate the expected BufferID, resolve a transient `NativeArray<T>` through `TryResolveHandle`, and assign the facade only after every lane meets capacity. The returned facade remains native views only; no persistent handle or local collection owner was introduced.
Rejected Alternatives: Leaving `GetBuffer<T>` because the facade is small was rejected because the migration target is the route, not only long-lived storage. Storing generation handles in `RetinalAdaptationVaultBuffers` was rejected because callers consume arrays and the facade should not retain descriptors across owner phases. Expanding into `PredatorCognitionDomain.cs` was rejected for this loop because that monolith contains broad `VaultArray<T>` debt across many cognition lanes and requires a separate blast-radius pass.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing retinal cadence behavior and bounded light capacity. Middle/high/ultra retain current retinal glare, blindness publication, and 300-frame telemetry behavior without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or AI cognition authority. The cheap retinal facade remains a data route; visual overkill still comes from downstream shader/cognition consumers, not extra facade math.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 cost is one descriptor validation per retinal lane when the facade resolves. Per-predator exposure updates, light source priority checks, blindness state publication, and telemetry ring writes remain descriptor-free after owner-phase binding.

## Decision 142 - Editor gizmos use registry Vault and descriptor read views
Problem: `Arm64AlignmentFaultGizmo.cs` and `MacroEcosystemHeatmapGizmo.cs` used `GlobalDataVault.TryGetLatestCreated` from ordinary editor gizmo drawing. `MacroEcosystemHeatmapGizmo.cs` also read sector, coordinate, and tuning lanes through direct `TryGetBuffer<T>` calls.
Solution: Route both gizmos through `GlobalRegistry.DataVault`. Keep the ARM64 fault gizmo on the existing `Arm64AlignmentTelemetry.TryGetNewestFault(IDataVault, ...)` API. Move macro heatmap sector/coord/tuning reads to method-local `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` descriptors resolved with `TryReadHandle`.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping latest-created because these are editor-only diagnostics was rejected because registry-published dependency is available and latest-created should remain bootstrap/crash fallback. Adding new macro heatmap cached handles was rejected because gizmos are read-only diagnostics and should not own ecosystem memory. Touching macro ecosystem runtime ownership was rejected because this pass only fixes the editor reader route.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep zero player-build cost because the draw paths are editor gated. Middle/high/ultra editor diagnostics retain heatmap and ARM64 fault visualization without changing macro ecosystem DTOs, BufferIDs, or alignment telemetry layout.
Hardware Impact: Runtime i3/MX350 cost is zero in player. Editor scene view pays one registry read per gizmo and three descriptor read validations for the macro heatmap before the existing bounded draw loop.

## Decision 143 - Fabrication smoke fallback registers the batch DataVault
Problem: `CraftingRuntimeSmokeTester.cs` created a fallback `GlobalDataVault` through `GlobalDataVault.Create` when batch mode had no latest-created Vault, but the fabrication runtime now resolves only `GlobalRegistry.DataVault`. A latest-only Vault would leave `FabricationAssemblerRuntime.EnsureRuntime()` without an authoritative registry Vault and break the fallback mock data generator expected by CI.
Solution: Replace the latest-created probe with `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` validation. In batch mode only, create the fallback Vault and immediately register it through `GlobalRegistry.RegisterDataVault` before mock fabrication generation.
Rejected Alternatives: Reintroducing latest-created fallback inside `FabricationAssemblerRuntime` was rejected because runtime owners must use the registry dependency route. Creating a Vault outside batch mode was rejected because it would hide missing bootstrap order in interactive/runtime contexts. Keeping the smoke tester as-is was rejected because it would silently fail the mock generator after the descriptor migration.
Scalability potential: Low devices and CI keep deterministic mock fabrication setup without touching gameplay truth. Middle/high/ultra retain the same mock job shape, shader payload route, telemetry shape, and continuous fabrication quality behavior once the registered Vault exists.
Hardware Impact: Player runtime cost is zero. Batch setup pays one unmanaged Vault creation only when no DataVault exists; fabrication mock generation then runs through the same registry-backed descriptor lanes as normal runtime.

## Decision 144 - Vault diagnostic visual probes use read descriptors
Problem: `VaultProbeUtility.cs` read existing buffers through direct `TryGetBuffer<T>`, and `VaultMemoryGizmoVisualizer.cs` used `GlobalDataVault.TryGetLatestCreated` plus direct `TryGetBuffer` reads for AUP and hot-entity lanes. These were diagnostics, but they still normalized direct buffer reads and latest-created polling in ordinary editor visualization.
Solution: Move `VaultProbeUtility` to `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>` plus `TryReadHandle`. Move `VaultMemoryGizmoVisualizer` to `GlobalRegistry.DataVault`, descriptor read views for `VaultAup64` and `VaultHotEntityData`, and a guarded concrete `GlobalDataVault` cast only for the existing telemetry snapshot API.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping direct reads because diagnostics are low-risk was rejected; diagnostics are long-lived editor consumers and must not train the codebase around stale routes. Adding cached handles to the gizmo was rejected because the SceneView draw path is read-only and should not own Vault descriptor state. Expanding `IDataVault` for `TryGetVaultTelemetrySnapshot` was rejected in this loop because it would touch core contracts for a diagnostic-only call.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep zero player-build cost for the editor gizmo and bounded diagnostic byte probing. Middle/high/ultra retain Vault memory visualization, swap-pop flash inspection, and raw byte probe capability without changing BufferIDs, telemetry layouts, or authority.
Hardware Impact: Runtime i3/MX350 cost is zero for editor-only gizmo paths. Diagnostic probes pay one descriptor read validation per inspected lane; the bounded gizmo loop and byte-span scan remain unchanged after view binding.

## Decision 145 - Metabolic control editor stops allocating physiology lanes from reads
Problem: `MetabolicControlCenterWindow.cs` used `GetBuffer<T>` to read tuning, decompression state, and Haldane coefficient lanes, and then wrote tuning directly through that mutable view. A UI refresh could allocate/grow gameplay physiology lanes and a slider edit bypassed explicit writer ownership.
Solution: Convert tuning and histogram reads to `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>` plus `TryReadHandle`. Convert slider commits to a `SystemID.CoreDiagnostics` writer fence with release in `finally`.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping editor `GetBuffer<T>` because designers need the window was rejected; read accessors must not allocate/grow buffers. Creating the physiology lanes from this editor was rejected because runtime physiology owns those facts. Migrating the larger physiology runtimes in the same loop was rejected because they contain broader handle/pointer debt and require a separate pass.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep zero player-build cost and cheap editor histograms. Middle/high/ultra retain designer physiology tuning and tissue/M-value visualization without changing physiology DTO layouts, BufferIDs, or runtime quality curves.
Hardware Impact: Runtime i3/MX350 cost is zero. Editor refresh pays descriptor read validation for tuning, state, and coefficient lanes; slider changes pay one diagnostics writer-fence transition.

## Decision 146 - Editor tuner windows stop normalizing stale Vault routes
Problem: Three small editor surfaces still normalized unsafe or impure Vault routes: `HabitatFluidIncursionTunerWindow.cs` read tuning/telemetry through `TryGetBuffer<T>` and wrote tuning directly, `HydrodynamicKccTunerWindow.cs` used `VaultBufferHandle<T>` plus `.Resolve(vault)`, and `NarrativeDagInspectorWindow.cs` polled `GlobalDataVault.TryGetLatestCreated` during normal editor drawing.
Solution: Convert fluid and KCC editor reads to `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>` plus `TryReadHandle`; convert tuning writes to `SystemID.CoreDiagnostics` writer fences released in `finally`; move the narrative inspector to `GlobalRegistry.DataVault`. KCC missing tuning lanes are created only on explicit write when allocation is not locked, not during read refresh.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping editor direct-buffer reads was rejected because editor windows are long-lived consumers and can outlive Vault relocation. Keeping read-time default writes in KCC was rejected because read accessors must not allocate/grow or mutate global state. Rewriting Quest DAG runtime handles in the same loop was rejected because that is a broad Quest-domain migration surface; this pass only removed the inspector's latest-created dependency.
Scalability potential: Low devices and player builds pay zero cost because these are editor-only windows. Middle/high/ultra retain designer controls, KCC environment visualization, fluid compartment bars, and narrative DAG inspection without changing gameplay truth, DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or continuous quality behavior.
Hardware Impact: Runtime i3/MX350 cost is zero. Editor reads pay one descriptor validation per displayed lane. Editor writes pay bounded writer-fence atomics only on changed sliders; no persistent `NativeArray<T>` fields or cached Vault pointers were introduced.

## Decision 147 - Cache B-Tree diagnostics and cold helpers use generation descriptors
Problem: `CacheBTreeTopologyXRayWindow.cs` still used `GlobalDataVault.TryGetLatestCreated` for editor tuning and telemetry reads, and the Cache B-Tree cold helper surface in `H8StaticDataContracts.cs` still allocated telemetry/tuning lanes through direct `GetBuffer<T>`. The direct routes bypassed descriptor freshness proof and kept ordinary diagnostics tied to latest-created fallback.
Solution: Route the editor window through `GlobalRegistry.DataVault`, read telemetry through `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>` plus `TryReadHandle`, and mutate tuning CSV imports through a `SystemID.CoreDiagnostics` writer fence released in `finally`. Convert cold helper allocation to `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, exact BufferID validation, and `TryResolveHandle` for method-local native views.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping latest-created in the editor was rejected because it is bootstrap/editor crash-only, not an ordinary draw/import dependency route. Keeping direct `GetBuffer<T>` in cold helpers was rejected because it normalizes mutable native views without generation proof. Migrating broader static-data bake/source APIs was rejected because this loop is limited to B-Tree Vault route debt and does not change binary payload identity.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same static-data B-Tree lookup cadence and editor-only diagnostic cost. Middle/high/ultra retain topology x-ray, traversal trace, telemetry waterfall, tuning CSV authoring, and B-Tree telemetry flush without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, Data Monolith identity, save identity, or `GlobalQualityWeight` authority.
Hardware Impact: Player runtime B-Tree lookup cost is unchanged. Cold setup pays one generation descriptor validation per telemetry/tuning lane; editor telemetry refresh pays one descriptor read validation; CSV import pays one diagnostics writer-fence transition.

## Decision 148 - Voxel sculptor editor tuning write uses descriptor writer fence
Problem: `ShinobuVoxelSculptorWindow.cs` wrote carve debris tuning into `CarveDebrisJobState` through direct `GetBuffer<int>` and legacy `TryLockBuffer`. The editor path was bounded, but it still bypassed generation freshness and writer-fence semantics.
Solution: Allocate/borrow `CarveDebrisJobState` through `GetGenerationHandle<int>`, validate exact BufferID, acquire a `SystemID.CoreDiagnostics` writer fence with `TryAcquireWriteLock`, call the existing `ShinobuDeltaCrusher.TryWriteCarveDebrisTuning`, and release the fence in `finally`.
Rejected Alternatives: Migrating `CarveDebrisComputeRenderer.cs` in the same loop was rejected because it contains a broad runtime handle/generation surface that needs a separate pass. Keeping `TryLockBuffer` was rejected because this editor authoring route can use the stricter descriptor writer API without changing runtime debris math.
Scalability potential: Low devices and player builds pay zero cost because this is editor-only authoring. Middle/high/ultra retain the same carve debris tuning ranges, CSV/binary authoring, and visual-overkill debris caps without changing `CarveDebrisJobState`, debris DTO layout, BufferIDs, or VFX authority.
Hardware Impact: Runtime i3/MX350 cost is zero. Editor save pays one descriptor validation and one writer-fence transition; debris simulation and indirect draw runtime paths are unchanged.

## Decision 149 - VR physical hand presence cold resolver uses method-local generation descriptors
Problem: `VRPhysicalHandPresenceIkJobs.cs` opened seven fixed hand-presence lanes through direct `GetBuffer<T>` calls. The job DTOs were already explicitly padded and the Burst job surface was deterministic, but the cold resolver still normalized direct mutable buffer allocation without descriptor freshness proof.
Solution: Add a generic method-local `TryResolveLane<T>` helper that allocates/borrows through `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, validates the exact BufferID, resolves through `TryResolveHandle`, and verifies capacity before returning the native view. Keep the existing output arrays and deterministic job ABI unchanged.
Rejected Alternatives: Persisting `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` fields was rejected because this static helper has no owner lifecycle and would create descriptor retention for no benefit. Rewriting the hand IK job was rejected because the hot job already consumes arrays and has explicit DTO layout sentinels; the defect was the cold Vault route.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing two-hand fixed packet, screen-space fallback, ghost-hand output, and 300-frame telemetry. Middle/high/ultra retain full surface/SDF projection, haptic scrape flags, AUP-local pose lanes, and blackbox telemetry without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, or gameplay authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays seven descriptor validations only when the resolver binds lanes. Per-hand IK math, AUP local projection, grab state update, and telemetry write loops remain descriptor-free after binding.

## Decision 150 - Leviathan terrain IK cold resolver uses method-local generation descriptors
Problem: `LeviathanTerrainIkJobs.cs` opened segment, bone, constraint, collider, telemetry, optional SDF, and optional heightmap lanes through direct `GetBuffer<T>` calls. The DTO layouts and deterministic terrain IK job were already explicit, but the cold resolver still bypassed generation freshness proof.
Solution: Add a method-local `TryResolveLane<T>` helper that allocates/borrows through `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, validates exact BufferID, resolves through `TryResolveHandle`, and verifies capacity. Preserve optional SDF and heightmap lanes by only resolving them when the requested count is greater than zero.
Rejected Alternatives: Persisting descriptors was rejected because the static resolver has no lifecycle authority and would create retained descriptor state. Rewriting terrain IK jobs was rejected because the hot Burst job already consumes local arrays, uses deterministic FloatMode, and carries explicit layout sentinels; the defect was only the cold Vault route.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep lower segment counts, terrain fallback, and optional zero-count SDF/heightmap lanes. Middle/high/ultra retain full segment capacity, SDF hugging, terrain height samples, collider proxy staging, tail-whip telemetry, and 300-frame blackbox output without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, or Animation/Fauna authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation per active lane only during resolver binding. FABRIK iterations, AUP-local terrain push, SDF/heightmap sampling, collider proxy writeback, and telemetry loops remain descriptor-free after binding.

## Decision 151 - Player/save route cleanup uses descriptor proof without changing gameplay truth
Problem: Four gameplay/save surfaces still used direct Vault access: `HectonPlayerState.cs` allocated player native lanes through generic `GetBuffer<T>`, `HectonPlayerMotor.cs` read `VoxelSdfTexture3D` through `TryGetBuffer<byte>`, `PlayerInventory.cs` read death-penalty rules through `TryGetBuffer<T>`, and `SaveManager.cs` allocated `WfcOutpostGrid` through `GetBuffer<byte>`. These routes bypassed generation proof in player movement, inventory, and save persistence seams.
Solution: Convert each route to method-local generation descriptors with exact BufferID validation and `TryResolveHandle` or `TryReadHandle`. Preserve fallback behavior in player motor by returning the published SDF payload when Vault descriptor proof fails.
Rejected Alternatives: Broadly migrating all player/inventory/save native surfaces was rejected because these files contain unrelated in-flight AUP/layout edits and much larger ownership surfaces. Keeping direct reads because some are cold was rejected because save, inventory, and movement seams are long-lived and rollback-sensitive.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing SDF fallback, player native lane capacities, inventory death-penalty rule route, and WFC save grid shape. Middle/high/ultra retain full voxel traversal, inventory rules, and WFC persistence behavior without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or gameplay authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at lane binding or command evaluation. Player kinematics, SDF traversal math, inventory rule loops, and WFC serialization remain unchanged after local view binding.

## Decision 152 - Atmosphere and bootstrap cold prewarm routes use descriptor proof
Problem: `GasDynamicsSolver.cs` allocated `HabitatBaseAwakeState` through direct `GetBuffer<byte>`, and `GameBootstrapper.cs` prewarmed `H8Time` and `RigidbodyAUPs` through direct `GetBuffer<T>`. These were cold paths, but they still trained boot/solver code around mutable native views without descriptor generation proof.
Solution: Convert atmosphere awake-state binding and bootstrap primary prewarm lanes to `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, exact BufferID validation, and `TryResolveHandle`. Keep bootstrap ordering, owner SystemIDs, and buffer lengths unchanged.
Rejected Alternatives: Migrating the larger gas solver and bootstrap unrelated in-flight changes was rejected because the worktree already contains separate hot-swap, SignalBus, AUP, shader warmup, and listener changes. Keeping direct cold prewarm calls was rejected because the pointer warden policy targets route normalization, not only hot paths.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same gas solver awake-state capacity, boot prewarm cadence, and Rigidbody AUP lane size. Middle/high/ultra retain gas toxicity/hibernation behavior, H8Time scheduling, and global physics AUP prewarm without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, bootstrap identity, or authority route.
Hardware Impact: Boot/solver binding pays one descriptor validation per lane. Gas diffusion, hibernation, toxicity signal emission, H8 time reads, and global physics AUP users remain unchanged after binding.

## Decision 153 - Global physics binding wrapper stores generation descriptors, not pointer handles
Problem: `GlobalPhysicsStateManager.cs` had a private `VaultBufferBinding<T>` wrapper that stored `VaultBufferHandle<T>`, allocated with `GetBufferHandle<T>`, and resolved through `Handle.Resolve(dataVault)`. That wrapper covered rigidbody AUPs, culling lanes, impact events, and physics telemetry, so stale pointer metadata could persist across physics phases.
Solution: Convert the wrapper to store `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, bind lanes through `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, validate exact BufferID and nonzero generation, and resolve local native views through `TryResolveHandle`. Preserve existing callers, implicit `NativeArray<T>` conversion, indexer shape, and undersized-buffer reacquire behavior.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting every physics callsite was rejected because the private wrapper already centralizes the route. Keeping `VaultBufferHandle<T>` was rejected because it violates the SHINOBU pointer-retention mandate. Broader physics manager changes were not touched because that file has unrelated in-flight AUP, listener, and SignalBus work.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep existing culling sleep lanes, awake results, impact-event capacity, and AUP arrays. Middle/high/ultra retain full physics culling telemetry, impact feedback, AUP jitter sentinel, and global physics state behavior without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, or physics authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation when a physics lane is resolved or reacquired. The culling job, impact flush, AUP update, and telemetry loops remain unchanged after local view binding.

## Decision 154 - Base module catalog helpers use descriptor proof
Problem: `BaseModuleCatalogRuntime.cs` allocated six catalog lanes and the hydration byte lane through direct `GetBuffer<T>`, read catalog views through direct `TryGetBuffer(...)`, and tagged telemetry with `IDataVault.VaultGenerationID`. The file is a cold/static-data helper, but it feeds construction catalog hydration, socket/cost queries, telemetry, and dump paths, so direct mutable views and broad Vault generation reads violate the pointer warden route.
Solution: Add method-local helpers that allocate/bind owned catalog lanes through `GetGenerationHandle<T>` plus exact BufferID validation and `TryResolveHandle`, read existing lanes through `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>` plus `TryReadHandle`, and record the telemetry lane descriptor generation instead of reading the global Vault generation property. Preserve all BufferIDs, capacities, binary endian policy, DTO layouts, and job inputs.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping direct calls because the catalog path is mostly cold was rejected; SHINOBU_202 is removing unsafe route normalization, not only hot-path cost. Persisting descriptors in static fields was rejected because these helpers have no owner lifecycle and only need phase-local binding. Rewriting binary hydration, hash lookup, or telemetry DTOs was rejected because the bounded defect was Vault access, not catalog math.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same mock catalog fallback, small hydration capacities, immutable binary payload route, and cheap socket/cost queries. Middle/high/ultra retain full construction module catalog hydration, topology lookup, telemetry ring, and blackbox dump behavior without changing Data Monolith identity, save identity, DTO layout, or Construction authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at catalog bind/read/hydration/telemetry seams. The Burst mock generation, binary hydration, hash lookup, socket range query, cost lookup, and telemetry ring writes remain local-array operations after binding.

## Decision 155 - Structural integrity borrows voxel SDF through read descriptors
Problem: `StructuralIntegrityCalculatorRuntime.cs` already owned its structural lanes through `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, but the solver borrowed `VoxelSdfTexture3D` through direct `_dataVault.TryGetBuffer(...)` before and after the solver lock window. That direct route bypassed descriptor freshness for a cross-domain voxel fact used by the SDF anchor job.
Solution: Add a method-local borrowed-lane reader that uses `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>`, exact BufferID validation, and `TryReadHandle`. Use it for both SDF presence detection and post-lock SDF binding before scheduling structural jobs.
Rejected Alternatives: Persisting a voxel SDF descriptor field was rejected because structural integrity is a borrower, not the voxel owner. Allocating/growing the SDF lane from structural integrity was rejected because voxel/SDF truth has a separate owner. Rewriting the solver jobs was rejected because they already consume local `NativeArray<byte>` input and the defect was only the borrowed lane route.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the same SDF fallback path when the texture lane is absent and the same continuous quality cadence. Middle/high/ultra retain full SDF anchoring, graph stress, collapse signals, shader upload, and 300-frame telemetry without changing voxel DTO layout, structural DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or Habitat/HullIntegrity authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor read validation before and after the existing lock decision. The SDF anchor job, graph stress job, collapse signal job, telemetry job, and shader upload operate on local views after binding, with no per-node descriptor cost.

## Decision 156 - Procedural crab IK facade stores generation descriptors
Problem: `ProceduralCrabLegIKRuntime.cs` persisted ten `VaultBufferHandle<T>` lanes, allocated them through `GetBufferHandle<T>`, and opened them through legacy `.Resolve(vault)` whenever runtime, registration, origin shift, telemetry, or GPU upload paths needed a view. The jobs were already data-oriented, but the facade carried stale pointer metadata across frames.
Solution: Convert the ten persistent lane fields to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, allocate with `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, and resolve all views through a single exact-BufferID helper using `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`. Existing `CrabLegVaultBuffers` native views and all scheduled job inputs are preserved.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting crab IK jobs was rejected because they already consume `NativeArray<T>` inputs with Burst and `[NoAlias]`; the unsafe defect was the facade route. Persisting local `NativeArray<T>` fields was rejected because it would create private native state outside the Vault. Touching unrelated hot-swap/scalability changes in this dirty file was rejected because those diffs preexisted this loop.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep two-leg raycast budgeting, corpse skip, bounded entity/leg capacities, and telemetry. Middle/high/ultra retain all-leg raycast mode, analytical two-bone IK, body tilt, indirect draw matrices, origin-shift rebase, and richer visual overkill without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or Animation/Fauna authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation only when binding the persistent view facade. Per-leg raycast build, target resolve, step scheduler, body tilt, analytical IK, AUP rebase, telemetry, and indirect draw upload loops remain descriptor-free after binding.

Touching unrelated hot-swap/registry-cache work in the dirty file was rejected because those diffs preexisted this loop.

Middle/high/ultra retain higher radial density, curl/noise visual overkill, acoustic echo taps, telemetry, CSV tuning, and procedural indirect beam rendering without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or VFX authority.

## Decision 159 - Wrist hologram HUD facade stores generation descriptors
Problem: `WristHologramHudRuntime.cs` persisted six `VaultBufferHandle<T>` lanes, allocated them through `GetBufferHandle<T>`, used legacy `.Resolve(vault)` for state/quads/font/telemetry/counters/acoustic taps, and exposed `GetHudStateAsRef` through handle `GetElementAsRef`. The UI job surface already works from local arrays, but the facade retained pointer-era metadata across frames, CSV/font reload, telemetry patching, blackbox dump, and draw upload.

Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting the text-to-quad Burst job or graphics upload path was rejected because those consumers already receive local arrays and GPU buffers; the unsafe defect was facade storage and legacy byref resolution. Persisting local `NativeArray<T>` views was rejected because it would create private native state outside the Vault. Moving the managed `Matrix4x4[]` draw payload was rejected because this loop targets stale Vault pointer routes only.

Middle/high/ultra retain richer glyph output, acoustic echo pings, telemetry, CSV font tuning, SDF shader intensity/glitch payload, and visual overkill without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or UI authority.

## Decision 160 - Voxel delta blackbox and scheduled carve lanes store generation descriptors
Problem: `VoxelDeltaProcessor.cs` persisted pointer-bearing handles for the 300-frame voxel carve blackbox ring and scheduled carve-write staging lane. The processor allocated those lanes through `GetBufferHandle<T>`, read handle `.Length/.IsCreated`, and opened local views through `.Resolve(vault)` across carve scheduling, commit, telemetry, and blackbox dump paths.
Solution: Convert both lanes to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`. Allocate with `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, validate exact BufferIDs, resolve local `NativeArray<T>` views through `IDataVault.TryResolveHandle`, and preserve the existing carve-write lock/unlock lifecycle around mutable writes.
