Problem: `ArchitectEyeVisualizer.cs` still opened runtime state, quad instance, signal telemetry, sector hash, and blackbox buffers through direct `IDataVault.GetBuffer<T>`, and sampled Voxel SDF density through direct `TryGetBuffer<byte>`. The hot-entity helper used generation handles but did not locally validate exact BufferID and required length before resolving.
Solution: Add descriptor fields for the owned Architect Eye lanes and central helpers `OpenOrAcquireVaultBuffer<T>`, `TryOpenExistingVaultBuffer<T>`, `TryOpenVaultBuffer<T>`, and `IsMatchingVaultHandle<T>`. Owned diagnostics lanes now reuse/acquire `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` descriptors; borrowed SDF and hot-entity lanes use existing-only descriptors and fail closed on mismatched BufferID, zero generation, unresolved handle, or short length.
Rejected Alternatives: Allocating missing Voxel SDF or hot-entity lanes from diagnostics was rejected because Architect Eye visualizes owner facts; it must not create shadow state. Rewriting the indirect quad renderer, glyph atlas, signal flow UI, or hot-swap service cache was rejected because the defect was stale Vault routes, not the diagnostic visual fake. Reverting existing hot-swap diffs was rejected because they preexisted this loop.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing continuous quad-capacity collapse, low entity/gas budgets, cheap SDF density proxy, and 300-frame blackbox. Middle/high/ultra retain richer entity labels, signal flow bars, sector map, gas heatmap, memory map, and indirect quad overkill without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or CoreDiagnostics authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation on slow-tick lane binding and existing-only SDF/hot-entity reads. Per-quad emission, signal telemetry copy, memory visualization, blackbox row write, and indirect buffer upload continue over local native views; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, or hidden `Complete()` was added.

## Decision 181 - Fauna residency facade removes pointer-era handles and byref access
Problem: `FaunaSimulationEngine.cs` kept `VaultBufferHandle<T>` fields for fauna pool slots, linear velocities, simulation flags, and free slots. The facade exposed `NativeArray<T>` properties through `.Resolve(vault)` and returned mutable refs through `GetElementAsRef`, preserving pointer-bearing handle semantics across frames. Teardown only cleared handles instead of releasing generation ownership.
Solution: Add `FaunaVaultBufferRoutes` for descriptor acquisition, exact BufferID/generation/length validation, release, and local-view ref extraction. Convert fauna residency/free-slot handles to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, acquire through `GetGenerationHandle<T>`, resolve through `TryResolveHandle`, and release through `IDataVault.ReleaseBuffer` before tombstoning.
Rejected Alternatives: Editing `FaunaDirector` call sites into a wider API refactor was rejected because the facade can preserve the existing property surface while removing stale pointer routes. Keeping `GetElementAsRef` was rejected because the old handle cached raw pointer metadata. Rewriting fauna LOD math or parasite attach jobs was rejected because they already receive local `NativeArray<T>` views and deterministic Burst directives.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain data-only LOD, hibernation/dehydration gates, cheap free-slot reuse, and no GameObject-heavy residency simulation. Middle/high/ultra retain larger resident slot capacity, richer velocity retention, and fauna visual overkill through downstream systems without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or AI/Fauna authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation when a local view/ref is requested from the facade. The scheduled `DataOnlyFaunaLodJob` still processes contiguous arrays with `[NoAlias]`; no extra job, GC allocation, or hidden main-thread completion was added.

## Decision 182 - Migration director double-buffer lanes use generation descriptors
Problem: `MigrationDirector.cs` persisted migration grid, blood-cloud POI, and swarm-state lanes as `VaultBufferHandle<T>`, resolved them through `.Resolve(vault)`, and swapped front/back legacy handles after each field rebuild. Refresh and allocate paths now open local views through `TryResolveHandle`; lock paths derive the write BufferID from the validated grid descriptor; shutdown releases descriptors through `IDataVault.ReleaseBuffer`.
Rejected Alternatives: Assigning one permanent BufferID to the front field was rejected because the director intentionally ping-pongs front/back grids. Rewriting migration field math, POI pruning, or swarm population logic was rejected because the defect was stale Vault route storage. Reverting existing runtime-AUP diffs was rejected because they preexisted this loop.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain cold-tick field rebuild cadence, coarse 100m migration cells, VR swarm population scaling, and cheap blood-cloud attraction. Middle/high/ultra retain denser migration grids, richer seasonal flow, swarm population overlays, and whale-fall bias without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or Ecosystem authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation during view refresh/allocation and lock preparation. The scheduled `BuildMigrationVectorFieldJob` still writes a contiguous back-grid array and keeps the existing dispatcher completion route; no new managed hot-path allocation or hidden completion was added.

## Decision 183 - Thermal dynamic resolution lanes stop retaining pointer handles
Problem: `ThermalDynamicResolutionAdapter.cs` persisted five `VaultBufferHandle<T>` fields, opened owned DRS/scale/telemetry lanes through `GetBufferHandle<T>`, read borrowed mock/scalability lanes through `TryGetBufferHandle<T>`, and dereferenced them with `ResolvePointer(...)`/`ResolveBuffer(...)`. Allocating mock reconstruction or scalability-state lanes from the adapter was rejected because those facts belong to their owning systems. Releasing owned lanes on `Dispose` was rejected in this loop to preserve the existing global DRS lane lifecycle.

Middle/high/ultra retain STP, FSR/TAA upscaler selection, shader visual-feature flags, dear-lie/overkill scalars, and telemetry without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or GraphicsScalability authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at lock/bind boundaries only. The EWMA Burst job, telemetry ring write, scale-state mirror update, shader global upload, and runtime snapshot publication still operate on local native views with no managed hot-path allocation and no hidden main-thread `Complete()`.

## Decision 197 - Physiology runtime stops retaining Vault pointer routes
Problem: `ShinobuPhysiologyRuntime.cs` retained twenty-one physiology lanes as `VaultBufferHandle<T>`, allocated them through `GetBufferHandle<T>`, checked legacy `.IsCreated`, and resolved every mock injector, editor accessor, simulation resolver, CSV parser, telemetry patch, signal publisher, and autopsy dump through `.Resolve(vault)`. That kept pointer-era route storage inside GameplayPlayer physiology, including gas-state and CSV scratch routes.
Solution: Convert every physiology Vault lane to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`. Add `OpenOrAcquirePhysiologyVaultBuffer<T>`, `OpenPhysiologyVaultArray<T>`, `OpenPhysiologyVaultBuffer<T>`, and `IsPhysiologyVaultHandle<T>`. GameplayPlayer-owned lanes now validate exact BufferID, `SystemID.GameplayPlayer`, nonzero generation, required length, successful `TryResolveHandle`, and `IsCreated` before any local native view is returned. Owner initialization is the only acquisition path; read/write helpers fail closed if the descriptor is missing.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting decompression math, gas toxicity math, tissue compartment defaults, CSV ingestion, blackbox dump format, signal payloads, or the job dependency graph was rejected because those systems are separate behavior surfaces. Keeping `.Resolve(vault)` for editor/mock paths was rejected because those routes are still executable stale-handle surfaces. Adding direct buffers for gas lanes was rejected because it would bypass the generation contract.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain bounded physiology rows, quality-weight active compartment scaling, mock depth/gas fallbacks, and cheap shader scalar publication. Middle/high/ultra retain richer gas toxicity, tissue telemetry, CSV tuning, signal output, and visual-overkill physiology shader payloads without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or GameplayPlayer authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at cold bind, mock/editor read-write, CSV, telemetry, signal publication, and simulation phase boundaries only. Physiology jobs still operate on local native arrays with the existing lock chain; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, or hidden main-thread `Complete()` was introduced by this route migration.

## Decision 198 - Spatial audio removes legacy Vault route APIs
Problem: `SpatialAudioManager.cs` retained multiple audio-owned and borrowed lanes as `VaultBufferHandle<T>`, used `GetBufferHandle<T>`, `ResolveBuffer`, `.Resolve(vault)`, direct `TryGetBuffer<byte>` for Voxel SDF, and a `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` fallback inside the Vault array helper. That kept stale route APIs in virtual voice sorting, acoustic portal data, radar grids, external scalability state, rollback audio suppression, and borrowed Voxel SDF.
Solution: Convert all spatial audio Vault handles to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`. Add `TryOpenAudioVaultBuffer<T>`, `TryOpenBorrowedAudioVaultBuffer<T>`, and `IsAudioVaultHandle<T>`. Audio-owned lanes validate exact BufferID, `SystemID.Audio`, nonzero generation, required length, successful `TryResolveHandle`, and `IsCreated`; borrowed lanes validate their actual owners: `SystemID.GraphicsScalability` for scalability state, `RollbackNetcodeVault.OwnerSystem` for rollback suppression, and `SystemID.WorldStreaming` for Voxel SDF.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting virtual voice sorting, acoustic portal A* scratch use, audio source residency, propagation delay, DSP output layout, or mixer policy was rejected because those are behavior surfaces outside this route migration. Keeping persistent `VaultBufferHandle<T>` only because the file still has native alias fields was rejected; it would leave direct stale-handle APIs in place. Fully removing every long-lived `NativeArray<T>` alias in this loop was rejected as a larger audio pipeline rewrite that must be split to avoid a compile wall.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain bounded virtual voice budgets, acoustic portal caps, cheap borrowed SDF fallback, and quality-weight audio policy. Middle/high/ultra retain richer virtual voices, acoustic path telemetry, radar upload, DSP material response, and audio overkill without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or authority ownership.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation during cache initialization, borrowed external refresh, and SDF handoff boundaries only. Existing audio jobs still consume current native views; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, or hidden main-thread `Complete()` was introduced. Residual risk remains: persistent `NativeArray<T>` alias fields should be converted to phase-local opens in a later audio-specific pass.

## Decision 199 - Tether cable route drops pointer-era Vault handles
Problem: `TetherInstance.cs` retained twenty-five Physics-owned Vault lanes as `VaultBufferHandle<T>`, opened them through `GetBufferHandle<T>`, resolved whole buffers and per-slot slices through `.Resolve(vault)`, and used `vault.VaultGenerationID` as a global shortcut before reusing long-lived native views. That left stale pointer-era route semantics in tow cable positions, Verlet solver scratch, visual spline output, tuning, tension forces, and blackbox telemetry.
Solution: Convert every tether Vault lane handle to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`. Add `OpenOrAcquireDataVaultCableBuffer<T>`, `TryOpenDataVaultCableBuffer<T>`, and `IsDataVaultCableHandle<T>`. Each route validates exact BufferID, `SystemID.Physics`, nonzero generation, required length, successful `TryResolveHandle`, and `IsCreated` before assigning a full native view or slot-local subarray. Remove the global `VaultGenerationID` shortcut so the bind path revalidates by per-buffer descriptor instead of global Vault epoch.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting the Verlet cable solver, tension/snap signal routing, visual bend-point fake, procedural GPU draw ABI, telemetry dump format, or slot reservation model was rejected because the defect was stale Vault provenance, not cable behavior. Keeping the global Vault generation check was rejected because per-buffer generation handles are the proof artifact required by SHINOBU_202. Fully eliminating all long-lived `NativeArray<T>` tether view fields in this loop was rejected as a larger phase-local view rewrite that must be split to avoid a compile wall.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain reduced Verlet segment counts, lower iteration budgets, taut-line visual fallback, bounded telemetry, and fixed slot capacity. Middle/high/ultra retain richer cable sag, more constraint iterations, GPU spline output, tension force publication, creak/snap signals, and visual-overkill material stress without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or Physics authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at the existing tether state bind/phase resolve boundary only. Verlet solve, visual spline build, tension publish, and telemetry writes continue over local native views with the existing job graph; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, same-frame readback, or hidden `Complete()` was added. Residual risk remains: persistent `NativeArray<T>` tether view fields should be converted to phase-local opens in a later cable-specific pass.

## Decision 200 - Tether AUP support routes use local generation descriptors
Problem: `TetherAupVerletJobs.cs` kept pointer-era helper routes even though its Burst jobs were already passed explicit native arrays. Telemetry introspection and dump paths used `TryGetBufferHandle` plus `.Resolve(vault)`, and mock bootstrap used `GetBufferHandle<T>().Resolve(vault)` for nodes, constraints, endpoints, spline vertices, force packets, pinned data, telemetry, material rows, CSV scratch, and bootstrap state.
Solution: Add `TetherAupVaultRoute`, a local descriptor helper that opens existing buffers or acquires them only when allocation is legal. Every helper validates exact BufferID, `SystemID.Physics`, nonzero generation, required length, successful `TryResolveHandle`, and `IsCreated` before returning a native view. Introspection and blackbox dump are read-only existing-descriptor paths; mock bootstrap is the only acquiring path.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting the deterministic Verlet jobs, AUP-local float conversion, mock endpoint generator, material row defaults, telemetry dump writer, or cable blackbox format was rejected because those behavior surfaces were not the stale route defect. Keeping direct handles in mock bootstrap was rejected because fallback CI data still runs code and must obey the same generation proof as runtime systems.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain cheap mock tether count, quality-weight iteration collapse, polynomial cable sway, and bounded 300-frame telemetry. Middle/high/ultra retain richer mock cable motion, higher iteration count, force packet output, material response, and blackbox proof without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or Physics authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation only in telemetry read/dump and mock bootstrap paths. Scheduled Burst jobs still receive the same native arrays and dependency handles; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, same-frame readback, or hidden `Complete()` was introduced.

## Decision 201 - Tether manager resolves Physics Vault lanes through descriptors
Problem: `TetherManager.cs` retained manager blackbox lanes as `VaultBufferHandle<T>`, used `VaultGenerationID` as a global generation shortcut, and resolved manager telemetry through `.Resolve(_dataVault)`. Its SHINOBU143 mock scheduler resolver also used `TryGetBufferHandle` and `.Resolve(_dataVault)` for AUP nodes, constraints, endpoints, segment tensions, solver stats, force packets, telemetry, pinned AUPs, and pinned mask.
Solution: Convert manager blackbox handles to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` and add local Physics descriptor helpers. Manager blackbox acquisition validates exact BufferID, `SystemID.Physics`, nonzero generation, required length, `TryResolveHandle`, and `IsCreated`; AUP scheduler reads open existing descriptors only. Generation-change handling now compares per-buffer descriptor generations instead of a global Vault epoch.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting tether rendering, indirect draw mesh setup, managed instance pooling, slow/fixed/late tick registration, mock AUP scheduling, or blackbox dump writer was rejected because those are behavior surfaces outside the stale route defect. Reusing the Tether AUP helper directly was rejected to keep the manager's route proof local and avoid hiding manager blackbox ownership behind a support-class name.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain limited active tethers, quality-tier visual bounds, cheap indirect segment impostors, and bounded 300-frame manager blackbox. Middle/high/ultra retain richer procedural tether visuals, more active instances, AUP mock scheduling, and blackbox forensic proof without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or Physics authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation when the manager resolves AUP buffers for scheduling or writes/dumps blackbox rows. Rendering buffers, active tether loops, and scheduled AUP jobs keep their existing flow; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, same-frame readback, or hidden `Complete()` was introduced.

## Decision 202 - Habitat fluid incursion removes pointer-era Vault routes
Problem: `HabitatFluidIncursionDirector.cs` retained Fluid-owned Vault lanes as pointer-era handles and still resolved multiple cold-init, topology, tuning, mass-signal, active/inactive buffer, telemetry, dump, and gizmo paths through `.Resolve(_vault)`. The migration also needed a concrete release path because generation descriptor acquisition increments Vault ownership and clearing descriptors alone would leak refcounts.
Solution: Convert every habitat-fluid Vault lane to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`. Add cached DataVault binding, hot-swap rebind, `OpenOrAcquireFluidVaultBuffer<T>`, `TryOpenFluidVaultBuffer<T>`, `ResolveFluidVaultBuffer<T>`, and `IsFluidVaultHandle<T>` helpers requiring exact BufferID, `SystemID.Fluid`, nonzero generation, required length, successful `TryResolveHandle`, and `IsCreated`. DataVault replacement and disable paths now complete pending simulation work, unlock job buffers, release each nonzero descriptor through the old Vault, and tombstone all local handles before reacquisition.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting fluid transfer, CSR topology, BFS flood containment, acoustic muffle publication, shader waterline upload, CSV hydration, or mock breach seeding was rejected because the behavior already uses a bounded cinematic flood model; the defect was stale Vault route storage and lifecycle hygiene. Keeping `.Resolve(_vault)` for editor gizmos or blackbox dumps was rejected because those routes still execute and can outlive Vault generation changes. Clearing descriptors without `ReleaseBuffer` was rejected because it would preserve a Vault refcount leak.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain the cheap compartment graph, quality-weight solver iteration collapse, bounded 300-frame telemetry, and shader waterline fake. Middle/high/ultra retain richer waterline shader payloads, acoustic flood muffle signals, mass-shift publication, and higher solver cadence without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, blackbox stride, or Fluid authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at cold bind, fixed/post-fixed/render/editor/CSV/mock/dump boundaries only. Flood solver jobs still consume local native arrays with the existing dependency chain; no managed hot-path allocation, tiny job, same-frame schedule/readback loop, or hidden `Complete()` was added by this route migration.

## Decision 203 - Physics force packet buffers use generation descriptors
Problem: `PhysicsApplySystem.cs` retained four force packet Vault lanes as `VaultBufferHandle<T>` and opened them through `GetBufferHandle<T>` plus `.Resolve(dataVault)`. That left pointer-era route storage inside the central physics force queue, front/back packet swap, validation packet copy, and validation mask paths.
Solution: Convert front/back force packet, validation packet, and validation mask handles to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`. `EnsureVaultBufferView<T>` now uses `GetGenerationHandle<T>` or `TryGetGenerationHandle<T>` under allocation lock, then validates exact BufferID, `SystemID.Physics`, nonzero generation, required length, successful `TryResolveHandle`, and `IsCreated` before exposing a native view. Shutdown now releases all four nonzero descriptors through `ReleaseBuffer(in handle)`; validation release uses the existing late-frame swap completion helper with `forceComplete: true` because the only release call is cold service shutdown.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting force packet semantics, rigidbody slot lookup, validation job layout, finite body recovery, contact modification, or queued force API was rejected because those are gameplay/physics behavior surfaces. Retaining legacy handles because the buffers are small was rejected because the central force queue is a high-authority stale route. Clearing descriptors without `ReleaseBuffer` was rejected because it leaks Vault refcounts.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain bounded packet capacity, finite-vector rejection, validation mask copying, and flush-budget clipping. Middle/high/ultra retain the same force queue, richer producer traffic, validation telemetry, and physics response without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or Physics authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation when packet buffers are ensured, read, cleared, swapped, or released. The front-buffer apply loop and validation Burst job still run over local native arrays; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, same-frame readback loop, or packet ABI change was introduced.

## Decision 204 - Submarine room mass SoA publish bridge uses descriptor wrappers
Problem: `SubmarineFluidDynamics.cs` still mirrored room water levels, room volumes, and room local AUPs into the shared Vault route through direct `TryGetBuffer` and `GetBuffer<T>` calls. That bypassed generation descriptor proof in a route consumed by ballast, rollback, construction stress, and cockpit visualization.
Solution: Add `VaultNativeBuffer<float>` / `VaultNativeBuffer<float3>` descriptor fields for the three shared room SoA lanes and publish through `Refresh`, `Ensure`, and `OpenView` instead of direct buffer APIs. The publish bridge preserves the old partial-buffer behavior: if no active compartments and no existing room SoA exists, it does not allocate; if a partial SoA exists or allocation is locked, all three lanes must already resolve. The local `VaultNativeBuffer<T>` wrapper now rejects descriptors whose `SystemID` is not `SystemID.VehiclesPhysics`.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting flood mass property calculation, submarine hydrodynamic state, room graph ownership, rollback descriptor layout, or consumer ballast code was rejected because those are separate authority surfaces. Allocating empty room SoA rows when no active compartments exist was rejected because the previous bridge avoided that cold allocation. Leaving direct `GetBuffer<T>` was rejected because it bypasses SHINOBU_202 generation proof.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain fixed room capacity and cheap post-fixed SoA publishing. Middle/high/ultra retain richer downstream ballast/rollback/stress/cockpit consumers without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or VehiclesPhysics authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor refresh/owner checks at the post-fixed publish bridge only. Flood mass jobs, hydrodynamic jobs, signal payloads, and consumer paths are unchanged; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, or hidden completion was introduced.

## Decision 205 - Equipment interaction queue drops pointer-era Vault handles
Problem: `EquipmentInteractionHandler.cs` retained four GameplayTools Vault lanes as `VaultBufferHandle<T>` and resolved interaction signal/raycast queue buffers through `ResolveBuffer`, `.Resolve(vault)`, and cached legacy handle `BufferId` fields. That left stale pointer-era route storage in the interaction signal queue, staged raycast command lane, scheduled command lane, and scheduled hit lane.
Solution: Convert all four lanes to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`. Add `EnsureInteractionVaultBuffer<T>`, `TryOpenExistingInteractionVaultBuffer<T>`, `IsGameplayToolsVaultHandle<T>`, and release helpers. Every route validates exact BufferID, `SystemID.GameplayTools`, nonzero generation, required length, successful `TryResolveHandle`, and `IsCreated` before reading, writing, clearing, scheduling, or completing raycast results. Shutdown and DataVault replacement complete pending scheduled raycasts, unlock scheduled lanes, release all nonzero descriptors through the owning Vault, and tombstone local handles before rebinding.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting interaction effect dispatch, raycast scheduling latency, collider side-channel arrays, platform-local hit rehydration, submarine heat routing, or organic/base contracts was rejected because those are behavior surfaces outside the stale Vault route defect. Keeping `BufferId` reads for locks was rejected because BufferID is static per lane and the descriptor must not be consulted before validation. Clearing descriptors without `ReleaseBuffer` was rejected because it leaks Vault refcounts.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain bounded 256 interaction signals, 64 frame-latent raycasts, async raycast completion, and existing cheap interaction side channels. Middle/high/ultra retain richer interaction traffic and visual/tool feedback without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, signal ABI, raycast queue layout, save identity, or GameplayTools authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation only at queue publish/read/clear, raycast stage/schedule/complete, cold bind, shutdown, and hot-swap boundaries. The scheduled raycast job still consumes the same local native command/hit arrays; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, same-frame readback, or hidden completion was introduced by this route migration.

## Decision 206 - Shader global slot cache uses per-buffer generation proof
Problem: `HectonShaderGlobalDataVaultBridge.cs` already stored the shader global slot lane as `VaultGenerationHandle<float4>`, but cache validation still read `vault.VaultGenerationID` into `_cachedVaultGeneration`. That keeps a whole-Vault epoch shortcut in a route whose actual authority proof is the `ShaderGlobalState` descriptor.
Solution: Remove `_cachedVaultGeneration` and the `vault.VaultGenerationID` read. The fast path now accepts the cached slots only when the cached Vault identity matches, the descriptor has exact GraphicsScalability ownership, and `TryResolveHandle(in _slotsHandle, out slots)` proves the current per-buffer generation.
Rejected Alternatives: Retaining the global epoch compare was rejected because it is broader than the fact being consumed and duplicates the descriptor generation check. Rewriting shader global slot layout or CBuffer publication was rejected because the defect was only cache proof provenance.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain the same 512-slot float4 shader bridge and fallback values. Middle/high/ultra retain richer shader scalar publication, physiology/noir/thermal telemetry slots, and visual-overkill shader payloads without changing slot indices, buffer length, shader property IDs, or GraphicsScalability authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 loses one whole-Vault generation read from the slot prepare path. `TryResolveHandle` remains the authoritative O(1) generation proof; no managed allocation, new job, shader variant, or GPU upload behavior changed.

## Decision 207 - Visor telemetry records descriptor generation, not whole-Vault epoch
Problem: `HectonVisorARStencilRendererFeature.cs` stored `vault.VaultGenerationID` in `_vaultGeneration` and wrote it into visor telemetry rows/dump headers. That field made the visor AR stencil route report a whole-Vault epoch instead of the actual generation of the UI-owned telemetry buffer.
Solution: Replace `_vaultGeneration` with `_telemetryDescriptorGeneration`, assigned from `_telemetryHandle.Generation` after the UI telemetry descriptor is acquired. Telemetry rows and dump headers continue using the existing `VaultGeneration` payload field, but the value now represents the owner-local telemetry buffer generation.
Rejected Alternatives: Changing `VisorTelemetryEntry` layout or dump header schema was rejected because binary payload layout is outside this route cleanup. Retaining `VaultGenerationID` was rejected because it violates one fact -> one owner -> one proof artifact; the proof is the telemetry descriptor, not the whole Vault.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain the existing visor HUD and AR stencil buffers. Middle/high/ultra retain richer AR target projection, HUD profile CSV, telemetry, and shader presentation without changing slot counts, telemetry stride, render pass behavior, or UI authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 removes one global epoch read from the cold ensure path. No managed allocation, new job, shader variant, render pass mutation, or GPU upload behavior changed.

## Decision 208 - Cavitation readiness proves descriptor lanes, not whole-Vault epoch
Problem: `AbyssalCavitationRuntime.cs` still used `_resolvedVaultGeneration == vault.VaultGenerationID` as the runtime-ready proof. That shortcut could make a VehiclesPhysics cavitation route look valid because the Vault instance was current, while the actual per-buffer descriptors for shockwaves, counters, entity snapshots, force packets, visuals, telemetry, ordnance profiles, CSV scratch, tuning, or SDF lanes were stale or owner-mismatched.
Solution: Remove `_resolvedVaultGeneration` and route readiness through `HasRuntimeDescriptorProof`. The proof checks all twelve cavitation descriptors by exact BufferID, `SystemID.VehiclesPhysics`, nonzero generation, required length, pure `TryReadHandle`, and `IsCreated`. Runtime and gizmo `OpenVaultView` helpers also reject non-VehiclesPhysics descriptors before resolving local views.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the global `VaultGenerationID` check was rejected because it is broader than the facts being consumed. Rewriting cavitation propagation, SDF dampening, shader visual upload, mock detonations, force transport, blackbox dump format, or ordnance CSV parsing was rejected because those were preexisting same-file behavior surfaces outside this route defect.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the bounded shockwave counts, quality-weight SDF sampling collapse, fixed 300-frame telemetry, and shader sphere fake. Middle/high/ultra keep richer SDF taps, visual-overkill cavitation shader payloads, force packet transport, and telemetry without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, shader slots, or VehiclesPhysics authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays twelve flat descriptor read checks at runtime-ready gates and one owner integer compare per local view open. Scheduled Burst jobs, graphics buffers, local NativeArray views, and dependency chaining are unchanged; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, hidden `Complete()`, or shader variant was introduced.

## Decision 209 - Biomimetic POI bridge keeps API stable but removes direct buffers
Problem: `ShinobuBiomimeticArchitectureRuntime.cs` exposed a small cold POI bridge that still read and allocated WorldStreaming Vault lanes through direct `TryGetBuffer` and `GetBuffer<T>`. Even without retained handles, those calls bypass SHINOBU_202 descriptor proof and train future bridge code to skip owner/generation validation.
Solution: Keep the public `NativeArray<T>` bridge API intact, but implement it with local `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` descriptors. Existing placement buffers open through `TryGetGenerationHandle` plus `TryReadHandle`; acquisition uses `GetGenerationHandle` only when allocation is legal, then validates exact BufferID, `SystemID.WorldStreaming`, nonzero generation, required length, and `IsCreated`.
Rejected Alternatives: Refactoring all POI bake jobs, editor dumps, and placement consumers to pass handles was rejected as unnecessary compile-wall expansion. Keeping direct `GetBuffer<T>` because the bridge is cold was rejected because the route still touches POI matrix payload ownership and DataVault generation discipline.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep bounded POI matrix capacity, emergency mock bounds, cheap visual-anchor sampling, HZB visible mask, and indirect-args fake. Middle/high/ultra keep richer POI placement, HLOD clustering, flora masks, HZB culling, and indirect draw arguments without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, or WorldStreaming authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one descriptor proof at POI bridge acquisition/read boundaries. Placement, HZB culling, indirect args, and dump Burst jobs still receive local native arrays; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, or hidden completion was introduced.

Keeping direct buffer calls because terrain seam work is slow-tick/editor-heavy was rejected because heightmap and blackbox payloads still need generation proof.

Middle/high/ultra retain richer hybrid projection, normal-aware blend masks, terrain seam telemetry, and visual-overkill terrain stitching without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, height payloads, or TerrainSeams authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at terrain signal ingestion, hybrid scratch acquisition, baseline refresh/read, and blackbox access only. ## Decision 211 - GI relay SH buffers stop retaining pointer-era Vault routes
Problem: `HectonGIRelaySystem.cs` retained six GraphicsScalability lanes as `VaultBufferHandle<T>` and opened SH profiles, SH output, lightning scratch, and telemetry ring through legacy `.Resolve(_vault)`. That left a pointer-bearing route in the day/night GI relay, lightning probe overlay, shader upload handoff, and 300-frame blackbox path.
Solution: Convert the six lanes to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`. Add `OpenGIRelayArray<T>`, `TryOpenGIRelayBuffer<T>`, `IsGIRelayVaultHandle<T>`, and release helpers requiring exact BufferID, `SystemID.GraphicsScalability`, nonzero generation, required length, successful `TryResolveHandle`, and `IsCreated` before any local native view is used. Cold disposal now completes pending SH work, releases all nonzero descriptors, tombstones local state, and then releases the graphics upload buffers.
Rejected Alternatives: Rewriting spherical harmonics profile generation, global shader state publication, water-volume cubemap binding, shadow cascade transitions, or the Burst SH lerp job was rejected because the behavior already uses a cheap lighting Dear-Lie: lerped SH coefficients and shader globals instead of CPU GI or per-surface lighting truth. Clearing descriptors without `ReleaseBuffer` was rejected because it leaks Vault ownership.

The SH lerp Burst job still receives local arrays with existing `[NoAlias]` fields and synchronous fast Burst flags; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, same-frame readback loop, hidden `Complete()`, shader variant, or DTO ABI change was introduced. Preexisting same-file removal of the `TryGetLatestCreated` fallback is not claimed by this decision.

## Decision 212 - Global shader dispatcher cache proves the slot descriptor only
Problem: `GlobalShaderDispatcher.cs` still cached `vault.VaultGenerationID` in `s_cachedVaultGeneration` and checked that whole-Vault epoch before reading the `ShaderGlobalState` slot buffer. That proof was broader than the fact consumed and duplicated the per-buffer descriptor generation already stored in `s_shaderSlotsHandle`.
Solution: Delete `s_cachedVaultGeneration` and every `VaultGenerationID` read from the dispatcher. `EnsureShaderGlobalSlots`, `TryResolveShaderGlobalSlotsLocked`, and `TryResolveCachedShaderGlobalSlots` now validate cached slot access through cached Vault identity plus `TryResolveShaderSlotsHandle`, which checks exact `BufferID.ShaderGlobalState`, `SystemID.GraphicsScalability`, nonzero descriptor generation, required length, and `TryResolveHandle` success.
Rejected Alternatives: Retaining the global epoch as an extra fast-path guard was rejected because it is not the owner-local proof artifact and can mask the real route contract. Rewriting global shader dispatch, slot constants, physiology payloads, wake fallback, thermal payloads, telemetry dump, or CSV override handling was rejected because those were preexisting same-file behavior surfaces outside this narrow cache-proof defect. Migrating scatter, dynamic point light, or interior GI retained handles in the same loop was rejected to avoid a compile-wall-sized edit.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain the existing single float4-slot shader bus, low-tier quality-weight shader parameters, mock thermal fallback, and reduced visual payload cost. Middle/high/ultra retain richer UberNoir feature masks, physiology shader payloads, thermal anomalies, and visual-overkill scalar dispatch without changing slot layout, BufferIDs, shader property IDs, or GraphicsScalability authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 removes one whole-Vault generation read/compare from shader slot cache validation. The dispatcher still performs one descriptor resolve for proof; command buffer dispatch, locks, telemetry writes, and graphics buffer uploads keep their existing path. No managed hot-path allocation, new job, hidden `Complete()`, shader variant, DTO layout change, or buffer ABI change was introduced.

## Decision 213 - GPU scatter renderer uses descriptor lanes without owning producer facts
Problem: `GpuScatterLodManager.cs` retained eight pointer-era `VaultBufferHandle<T>` fields and used `ResolveBuffer`, `.Resolve(vault)`, `ResolvePointer`, `TryGetBufferHandle`, and `TryGetBufferGeneration` for flora matrices, metadata, auxiliary shader lanes, blackbox rows, and CPU audit buffers. The renderer file also declares that OSHINO or another producer may write the matrix/metadata facts, so blindly releasing all descriptors on shutdown would turn a stale-pointer cleanup into a cross-domain ownership bug.
Solution: Convert the eight retained lanes to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` and add local scatter helpers requiring exact BufferID, `SystemID.Vfx`, nonzero generation, required length, `TryResolveHandle`, and `IsCreated` before any native view is used. Blackbox recording/dump now writes through a resolved `NativeArray<ScatterBlackBoxEntry>` instead of `ResolvePointer`. Renderer-owned blackbox and CPU audit scratch descriptors are released on disable/destroy/hot-swap; the producer handoff lanes are tombstoned locally only.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the legacy bridge was rejected because GPU scatter is a long-lived renderer and can survive Vault relocation. Releasing `FloraScatterMatrices`, `FloraScatterMetadata`, `FloraScatterAge01`, `FloraScatterPhaseSeeds`, and `FloraScatterVisualPayload` from this renderer was rejected because the file-level producer contract says another authoring system may own those facts. Rewriting the compute cull kernel, indirect draw args, flora shader payload, or high-tier subsurface visual parameters was rejected because those are behavior/render surfaces outside the stale route defect.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain bounded cull distance, stress shedding, shader-buffer flora presentation, and optional CPU audit off by default. Middle/high/ultra retain richer visual payload, anisotropic subsurface scalar lanes, GPU indirect flora, and optional Burst audit without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, shader property IDs, or Vfx route ownership.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at bind, upload, blackbox, and manual audit boundaries only. GPU indirect draw, compute culling, graphics buffer upload, and shader material payloads keep their existing work distribution. No managed hot-path allocation, new job, same-frame readback loop, hidden `Complete()`, shader variant, or DTO ABI change was introduced by this route migration.

## Decision 214 - Dynamic point-light culling drops pointer-era Vault lanes

Middle/high/ultra retain richer near-field boost, probe bounce rows, sort precision, and visual-overkill dynamic lighting payloads without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, shader property IDs, or GraphicsScalability authority.

## Decision 218 - Vehicle motor shared lanes stop retaining pointer handles
Problem: `VehicleMotor.cs` retained three VehiclesPhysics lanes as `VaultBufferHandle<T>` and opened submarine state, scheduled capsule sweep commands, and scheduled capsule sweep results through `GetBufferHandle`, legacy `.Resolve(_dataVault)`, retained handle `.IsCreated`, `GetElementAsRef`, and a stale `GlobalDataVault.TryGetLatestCreated` fallback. ## Decision 220 - Seed Ship anomaly releases only its EndgameAnomaly descriptors
Problem: `SeedShipAnomalyRuntime.cs` retained thirteen Vault lanes. The scalability descriptor is acquired with `TryGetGenerationHandle<ScalabilityStateDTO>`, verified as `SystemID.GraphicsScalability`, read through `TryReadHandle`, and never released by SeedShip.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping `.Resolve(vault)`, `GetBufferHandle`, `TryGetBufferHandle`, and retained handle `.IsCreated` was rejected because anomaly state survives across jobs, CSV reloads, telemetry dumps, and DataVault replacement. Releasing `ShinobuScalabilityState` from SeedShip was rejected because GraphicsScalability owns that continuous quality fact. Rewriting anomaly field math, leviathan fake behavior, shader bridge, legacy binary ingest, CSV parser, or SignalBus payloads was rejected because the defect was stale Vault provenance, not EndgameAnomaly behavior.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain the existing cheap anomaly field, bounded mock leviathan count, CSV tuning, and fallback quality-weight path. Middle/high/ultra retain richer corruption intensity, radar/Babel/glitch shader scalars, mock rebase pulses, and visual-overkill anomaly presentation without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, signal ABI, shader property IDs, or GlobalQualityWeight authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at cold ensure, read accessor, job schedule/finalization, legacy `.h8bin` ingest, CSV reload, telemetry dump, and DataVault-rebind boundaries only. Existing SeedShip jobs still consume local native arrays and the borrowed quality read remains a pure descriptor read; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, same-frame readback loop, hidden completion beyond teardown/finalization fences, shader variant, DTO ABI change, or authority route change was introduced.

## Decision 221 - Flora genome facade stores capacity proof with descriptors
Problem: `FloraGenomeVaultRuntime.cs` retained twelve FloraGenomics Vault lanes through pointer-era handles. Rejected Alternatives: Keeping `GetBufferHandle` and `.Resolve(_vault)` was rejected because async binary ingest, CSV reloads, chunk workspaces, scheduled L-system jobs, and blackbox dumps can outlive a Vault relocation window. Proving only length `1` for matrix/hazard/genome lanes was rejected because it proves the header, not the bound working set. Rewriting the binary reader, L-system job ABI, CSV hotloader, hardware-tier enum, or blackbox dump schema was rejected because this loop targets stale Vault provenance only.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain bounded genome, branch matrix, hazard, turtle stack, CSV scratch, and 300-frame blackbox capacities. Middle/high/ultra retain richer L-system expansion, larger matrix/hazard budgets, CSV tuning, and visual-overkill flora spawning without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, binary `.h8bin` format, SignalBus payloads, or FloraGenomics authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at bind, workspace creation, async raw-byte lock, CSV reload, generation schedule/finalization, binary decode, and dump boundaries. L-system Burst jobs still consume local native views with the existing dependency chain; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, hidden completion, DTO ABI change, shader variant, or DataVault global polling was introduced.

Solution: Convert all retained lanes to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`. Add MarineSnow-local helpers requiring exact BufferID, `SystemID.Vfx`, nonzero generation, required length, successful `TryResolveHandle` or pure `TryReadHandle`, and `IsCreated`. Owned lanes are acquired with `GetGenerationHandle` and released through `ReleaseBuffer(in handle)` during disable/destroy/DataVault replacement. `WakeSources` is borrowed with `TryGetGenerationHandle`, validated as an existing VFX descriptor, and only tombstoned locally. The compaction fence now marks native state not-ready and drops only the borrowed alias rather than discarding owned release provenance.
Rejected Alternatives: Releasing `WakeSources` from Marine Snow was rejected because `FloraInteractionManager` owns and publishes that bridge fact. Keeping pointer handles because the jobs are immediate `Run()` calls was rejected because stale views can still cross CSV reload, graphics upload, telemetry dump, and DataVault replacement boundaries. Rewriting compute shader advection, sonar glow, fog injection, propwash ring math, CSV parsers, blackbox schema, or indirect draw arguments was rejected because this loop targets stale Vault provenance only.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain continuous GlobalQualityWeight particle-capacity collapse, low-resolution sonar glow, cheap fog-density injection, bounded mock wake/prowash events, and shader-driven silt fakes. Middle/high/ultra retain richer marine-snow density, propwash event richness, dynamic wake upload, GPU fog/glow presentation, and visual-overkill shader response without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, compute kernel ABI, shader property IDs, signal ABI, telemetry dump format, or Vfx authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at native-state ensure, silt tuning reads, mock flow writes, dynamic wake upload, propwash event commit, borrowed wake-source harvest, telemetry writes, blackbox dump, and CSV reload boundaries only. Existing immediate Burst jobs retain synchronous fast Burst flags and `[NoAlias]` arrays; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, hidden `.Complete()`, same-frame schedule/readback loop, shader variant, DTO ABI change, graphics buffer ABI change, or hot GlobalRegistry polling was introduced. `HectonMarineSnowRenderer.cs` is reported as untracked by Git in this workspace, so this decision claims the on-disk runtime edit, not repository index state.

## Decision 228 - Somatic kinematics releases GameplayPlayer descriptors instead of retaining pointers
Problem: `SomaticKinematicsRuntime.cs` retained nine GameplayPlayer lanes as `VaultBufferHandle<T>` and opened player kinematic state, bounding sphere, hand stroke history, tuning, drag LUT, signal scratch, blackbox ring/cursor, and CSV scratch through `GetBufferHandle`, `.Resolve(vault)`, retained handle created/length checks, and `GetElementAsRef`. Owned acquisition uses `GetGenerationHandle` only. Disable, destroy, DataVault replacement, and cold service rebind complete pending jobs, unlock active lanes, release all nine nonzero descriptors through `ReleaseBuffer(in handle)`, and tombstone route state. `GetStateRef` now resolves a phase-local native view and returns a byref through `UnsafeUtility.ArrayElementAsRef`, eliminating the retained-handle byref helper. `SomaticKinematicsJob` fields now carry `[NoAlias]`.
Rejected Alternatives: Acquiring owned lanes through `TryGetGenerationHandle` fallback was rejected because ownership/refcount release becomes ambiguous for a single-player GameplayPlayer owner. Rewriting swimming thrust, SDF pushout, surface buoyancy, VR hand sampling, acoustic/haptic publish content, binary tuning probe, CSV parser, blackbox schema, or quality-tier behavior was rejected because this loop targets stale Vault provenance only.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain existing cheap mock SDF, short hand-history ring, deterministic emergency tuning, bounded drag LUT, fixed 300-frame blackbox, and low-tier signal capacity. Middle/high/ultra retain richer seaglide/stroke response, CCD step budget, acoustic/haptic feedback, CSV-tuned drag profiles, and blackbox diagnostics without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, save identity, SignalBus ABI, or GameplayPlayer authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at cold ensure, job buffer lock/resolve, origin-shift patch, tuning write, CSV scratch load, signal publish, blackbox dump, and mutable state-ref boundaries only. Existing deterministic Burst job scheduling, dependency completion through `DispatcherJobSwap`, AUP-local math, CSV `ReadOnlySpan<byte>` parser, and blackbox dump format remain unchanged; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, same-frame readback loop, hidden completion outside existing teardown/post-fixed fences, shader variant, DTO ABI change, or new hot GlobalRegistry polling was introduced.

## Decision 229 - VR Somatic provider wrapper stops hiding pointer-era Vault handles
Problem: `VRSomaticProvider.cs` and `VRSomaticProvider.Comfort.cs` centralized eighteen GameplayPlayer Vault lanes behind `VaultNativeArray<T>`, but that wrapper retained `VaultBufferHandle<T>`, exposed `Resolve()`, and all lane creation still called `GetBufferHandle`. The provider schedules head capsule casts, root sync, hand kinematics, comfort seed/kernel jobs, writes shader globals, and dumps blackbox data; stale wrapper handles could survive DataVault replacement because the provider was not registered for GlobalRegistry hot-swap.
Solution: Convert `VaultNativeArray<T>` to hold `VaultGenerationHandle<T>`, BufferID, required length, and the owning Vault reference. Creation now calls `GetGenerationHandle` with `SystemID.GameplayPlayer`; reads use `TryReadHandle`; mutable views use `TryResolveHandle`; direct `.Resolve()` call sites were renamed to `AsNativeArray()` to remove pointer-era semantics. Disable/inactive/destroy release paths call `ReleaseBuffer(in _handle)` through each wrapper after pending jobs are completed. The provider now implements `IGlobalRegistryHotSwapListener`; DataVault replacement disposes/reacquires at the callback boundary.
Rejected Alternatives: Placing DataVault identity checks and forced disposal inside `ResolveDataVault` was rejected because `Resolve*`/read accessors must not publish, complete jobs, release buffers, or mutate global state. Keeping `VaultNativeArray.Resolve()` as a compatibility name was rejected because it normalized pointer-era semantics and would keep stale route scans noisy. Rewriting root-sync horizon math, comfort profile blending, capsule sweep jobs, hand ghosting, shader publication, or blackbox schema was rejected because this loop targets stale Vault provenance only.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain existing low-capacity head-cast batch, bounded two-hand kinematics, comfort pressure fallback, comfort profile LUT, and fixed blackbox frame count. Middle/high/ultra retain richer root horizon correction, comfort kernels, haptic/acoustic feedback, shader state publication, and telemetry diagnostics without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, shader property IDs, SignalBus/GlobalSignals ABI, or GameplayPlayer authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at buffer ensure, wrapper indexing, job setup, comfort seed/kernel setup, blackbox writes/dump, and DataVault hot-swap boundaries only. Existing Burst job graph, CapsulecastCommand batch, shader scalar upload, comfort CSV parser, and GlobalRegistry service route remain unchanged; no managed hot-path allocation, new per-frame scene search, same-frame readback loop, hidden completion inside a read accessor, shader variant, DTO ABI change, or direct sibling-domain dependency was introduced.

## Decision 231 - Quest DAG descriptors keep narrative state relocatable
Problem: `QuestDagRuntimeTypes.cs` and `QuestDagResolverRuntime.cs` retained sixteen QuestDag lanes as pointer-era handles. The resolver opened state masks, node DTOs, runtime DTOs, trigger volumes, item/faction SOA lanes, telemetry, counters, trigger index buffers, and CSV monitor through `GetBufferHandle`, `.Resolve(vault)`, `ResolvePointer`, and retained handle length/created checks. Those handles can outlive Vault relocation across scheduled resolver jobs, save-copy bridges, editor force-complete operations, OSHINO/mock data hydration, CSV overrides, and deadlock telemetry dumps.
Solution: Convert every retained Quest DAG lane to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` and store the required capacity proof inside `QuestDagBufferHandles`. `QuestDagVault.TryResolveBuffers` now requires exact BufferID, `SystemID.QuestDag`, nonzero generation, successful `TryResolveHandle`, `IsCreated`, and capacity length before it returns frame-local `NativeArray<T>` views. `GetStateMaskRef` resolves a local state-mask view before deriving the byref through `UnsafeUtility.ArrayElementAsRef`, removing the raw `ResolvePointer` path. `ReleaseBuffers` releases all sixteen QuestDag-owned descriptors through `ReleaseBuffer(in handle)` after jobs are complete. The synchronous `Dispose()` path completes active resolver work before release; the nonblocking `Dispose(JobHandle)` path preserves the dependency fence and releases only when no active resolver job is pending. Burst job fields now carry `[NoAlias]`.
Rejected Alternatives: Releasing QuestDag buffers under an active nonblocking dispose dependency was rejected because scheduled jobs already hold frame-local native views. Keeping legacy handles because the data is narrative rather than physics was rejected because stale pointer UAF is domain-independent. Rewriting fixed-point DAG logic, AUP trigger math, OSHINO binary parsing, CSV span parser, emergency mock generator, SignalBus emission, or editor UI text was rejected because this loop targets stale Vault provenance only.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain existing tick dilation, bounded no-trigger pass budget, deterministic emergency mock graph, compact bitmask state, and fixed 300-frame telemetry. Middle/high/ultra retain richer node/trigger capacities, editor DAG inspection, CSV tuning, blackbox diagnostics, and visual-overkill narrative reactions without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, OSHINO binary format, save identity, SignalBus ABI, or QuestDag authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at buffer ensure, OSHINO/mock load, CSV override, job schedule, save copy, editor force-complete, state-ref mutation, and telemetry dump boundaries only. Existing Burst DAG jobs still consume local native views with synchronous fast Burst flags; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, same-frame readback loop, shader variant, DTO ABI change, or hot GlobalRegistry polling was introduced. `IDisposable.Dispose()` can block only as an explicit teardown path; the dependency-returning overload keeps the nonblocking scheduler contract.

## Decision 233 - QA watchdog descriptors survive navigation and writer fences
Problem: `Shinobu38QaWatchdogRuntime.cs` retained sixteen pointer-era Vault handles and opened them through `GetBufferHandle`, `.Resolve(vault)`, handle `.IsCreated`, `GetElementAsRef`, and `GetElementAsReadOnlyRef`. The watchdog schedules a navigation job over seven lanes, runs a background SPSC file writer over command/payload/cursor lanes, ingests waypoint CSV bytes, and writes 300-frame dumps. Replace byref helper calls with `ElementRef` over already-resolved local views. `OnDestroy` preserves fence order: force-complete the navigation job, stop/join the writer thread, unregister ticks, unlock the 16 runtime buffer IDs, release all nonzero descriptors through `ReleaseBuffer(in handle)`, and tombstone local handles.
Rejected Alternatives: Releasing descriptors before `StopFileWriter()` was rejected because the writer thread keeps cursor/command/payload views alive until the join returns. Keeping the input-current bridge on legacy handles was rejected even though `BufferID.ShinobuInputCurrentDto` ownership can mirror existing Vault metadata; descriptor release still matches the acquired refcount. Rewriting the QA file writer, profiler recorder sampling, result JSON schema, waypoint CSV parser, automation input payload, or continuous quality torture curve was rejected because this loop targets stale Vault provenance only.

Middle/high/ultra retain richer quality recovery proof, waypoint override ingestion, crash dumps, and QA artifact fidelity without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, file schemas, SignalBus payloads, AUP-local math, or watchdog authority route.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one descriptor validation at startup clear, FastTick scheduling, LateFrame consumption, CSV queueing, writer-thread drain, waypoint ingest, debug facade read, and telemetry dump boundaries. Existing Burst navigation job still consumes local native views with `[NoAlias]`; no new hot managed allocation, hidden same-frame readback, shader variant, DTO ABI change, or build invocation was introduced.

## Decision 234 - Player kinematics lockstep and SDF reads stop using direct Vault views

Releasing or rewriting all `VaultBufferBinding<T>` lanes was rejected in this loop because the prior pass explicitly preserved descriptor-clear-only ownership semantics; changing that refcount behavior across fifteen player kinematics lanes needs a separate ownership audit. Rewriting SDF squeeze math, AUP conversion, hand IK ray commands, telemetry dump schema, KCC authority, or SignalBus payloads was rejected because the defect was the resurfaced direct Vault route.

Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at SDF payload fallback, player-state pre-squeeze read, and player-state write boundaries only. No managed hot-path allocation, new job, same-frame schedule/readback loop, shader variant, DTO ABI change, build invocation, or new hot GlobalRegistry polling was introduced.

## Decision 239 - Save pager worker fences descriptors before release
Problem: `H8BinaryWorldPager.cs` retained nine SavePersistence Vault lanes as pointer-era handles and opened them through `GetBufferHandle`, `.Resolve(vault)`, and retained handle created checks. The pager also had a public staging-slice route that polled `GlobalRegistry.DataVault` directly and a DataVault hot-swap path that could release descriptors even when the worker thread failed to stop.
Solution: Convert retained pager lanes to `VaultGenerationHandle<T>` descriptors with local helpers requiring exact BufferID, `SystemID.SavePersistence`, nonzero generation, positive required length, inactive compaction fence, descriptor read/resolve, and `IsCreated`. Acquisition now rejects allocation-lock and compaction-fence windows, readiness-proofs all nine lanes, and releases partial acquisitions on failure. Teardown unregisters the hot-swap listener and releases owned descriptors through `ReleaseBuffer(in handle)`. DataVault replacement now sets the dispose request, waits for the worker, and returns without releasing if the worker is still alive; only a fenced worker path dumps telemetry, clears transient queues, releases old-vault descriptors, and tombstones `_vault`.
Rejected Alternatives: Releasing descriptors while the worker thread may still be inside `ProcessWrite`, `ProcessRead`, telemetry dump, or queue drain was rejected because it creates the exact UAF/refcount failure this batch is removing. Changing the public `TryReadPageIntoVaultSlice(... out VaultBufferSlice<byte> ...)` signature to a pointer-free slice descriptor was rejected in this loop because AGENTS forbids public API mutation during a batch without an explicit route card; the existing compatibility route now uses cached `_vault` and rejects compaction/allocation fences. Rewriting WAL layout, page headers, RLE compression, FileStream/MMF behavior, or directory-page math was rejected because this loop targets Vault provenance only.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain bounded queue capacity, RLE-as-cheap-compression, fixed 300-frame telemetry, write-ahead safety, and staging-slice fallback without extra hot registry polling. Middle/high/ultra retain the same binary save format, WAL replay, hot-state staging, MMF commit path, richer page telemetry, and visual-overkill world streaming consumers without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, page identity, WAL identity, or SavePersistence authority.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at init, queue enqueue/dequeue, worker IO, hot-state staging, telemetry record/dump, and DataVault replacement boundaries only. The worker still operates over phase-local native views; no managed hot-path allocation, new job, hidden same-frame job completion, shader variant, binary payload format change, save identity change, or hot GlobalRegistry polling was introduced. The public raw slice route remains compatibility debt but is now allocation/compaction fenced.

## Decision 240 - Diegetic glitch terminal bridge uses mutable descriptor resolve
Problem: `TryPushTerminalStateGlitch` holds the UI write lock and mutates `TerminalStateDTO` rows, but opened the borrowed terminal state lane through `TryReadGlitchVaultBuffer`. That violated the doctrine that read accessors are pure and must not be used as mutation routes.
Solution: Change the already-locked Terminal OS state bridge open from `TryReadGlitchVaultBuffer` to `TryResolveGlitchVaultBuffer`. The bridge still borrows `TerminalOsStateBridgeBufferId` through `TryGetGenerationHandle<TerminalStateDTO>`, validates exact BufferID, `SystemID.UI`, nonzero generation, required length, and compaction-fence absence, and never releases Terminal OS owned descriptors.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping the pure read route was rejected because the route writes `Value2` and `IsDirty`. Rewriting Terminal OS, shader upload, glitch jobs, CSV, blackbox, or `TerminalStateDTO` layout was rejected because this loop targets the bridge access-mode defect only. Changing the public bridge ABI was rejected.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the existing continuous quality-weight cadence for terminal glitch pushes. Middle/high/ultra keep richer glitch frequency and shader response without changing DTO layout, BufferID, save identity, or UI ownership.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 cost is unchanged: one descriptor resolve at a late-frame bridge boundary under an existing lock. No managed allocation, new job, hidden completion, shader variant, direct sibling dependency, or hot GlobalRegistry polling was introduced.

## Decision 241 - System dispatcher bridge probes require generation descriptors
Problem: `SystemDispatcher.cs` still used direct `TryGetBuffer` for rollback runtime state and Vault address-shift records. The rollback probe uses BufferID `70752`, which is also named by unrelated source routes, so a direct buffer view can silently reinterpret memory if the wrong owner occupies that ID. The address-shift route also mutates the count row after publishing, so direct read/grow semantics are the wrong authority path.
Solution: Add dispatcher-local existing-buffer helpers. `TryReadExistingDispatcherVaultBuffer` validates exact BufferID, expected owner `SystemID`, nonzero generation, compaction-fence absence, required length, and pure `TryReadHandle`. `TryResolveExistingDispatcherVaultBuffer` applies the same descriptor proof but uses mutable `TryResolveHandle`. Rollback state uses `SystemID.CoreDeterminism`; Vault address-shift count/records use `SystemID.CoreDataVault`.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping direct `TryGetBuffer` was rejected because it bypasses owner/type proof and can hide BufferID collisions. Allocating or growing missing buffers from the dispatcher was rejected because these are borrowed facts owned by CoreDeterminism or CoreDataVault. Importing networking constants was rejected to avoid adding another direct compile dependency to this already-wide core file.
Scalability potential: Low devices get deterministic false-return behavior instead of corrupt reinterpretation. Middle/high/ultra keep relocation-signal richness and rollback visual suppression without changing payload shape, authority route, or gameplay truth.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one descriptor validation at visual-sync rollback fence and post-defrag address-shift publish/reset only. No managed allocation, new job, lock, hidden completion, DTO ABI change, SignalBus ABI change, shader variant, or hot GlobalRegistry polling was introduced.

## Decision 242 - Headless fracture bot borrows Rigidbody AUPs through a read descriptor
Problem: `HeadlessStressFractureBot.cs` scanned `BufferID.RigidbodyAUPs` through direct `vault.TryGetBuffer<double3>`. The bot is a QA consumer looking for NaN poisoning, not the owner of physics AUP storage. Direct buffer access bypassed owner proof and could normalize future direct Vault reads inside diagnostic code.
Solution: Add `TryReadRigidbodyAupBuffer`. It requires an existing `VaultGenerationHandle<double3>` for `BufferID.RigidbodyAUPs`, exact `SystemID.GlobalPhysicsStateManager`, nonzero generation, compaction-fence absence, `TryReadHandle`, created buffer, and nonzero length before the bot scans the phase-local native view.
Rejected Alternatives: Allocating or growing the Rigidbody AUP buffer from the QA bot was rejected because ownership belongs to `GlobalPhysicsStateManager`. Removing the bot's source-audit string literals for `TryGetBuffer<` / `TryGetBufferHandle<` was rejected because those strings are detector inputs, not executable Vault routes.
Scalability potential: Low devices keep the cheap QA scan miss path when physics AUPs are absent or fenced. Middle/high/ultra keep full NaN forensics without changing physics DTO layout, BufferID, rollback truth, or AUP precision.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays one descriptor validation per QA Rigidbody AUP scan, then the existing contiguous loop over `double3` values. No managed allocation, new job, hidden completion, DTO ABI change, SignalBus ABI change, shader variant, or hot GlobalRegistry polling was introduced.

## Decision 243 - Vault sovereignty maintenance no longer marks its own buffers as external views
Problem: `VaultSovereigntyMaintenance` is the CoreDataVault owner path for AUP wrapping, active entity counts, sector-local mirrors, CSV scratch, and address-shift rows, but it still opened those buffers through direct `GetBuffer` and `TryGetBuffer`. In `GlobalDataVault`, direct buffer opens expose an external view and can mark a block as non-relocatable. That defeats the relocation/sovereignty proof this code exists to maintain.
Solution: Add `TryEnsureCoreVaultBuffer`, `TryResolveCoreVaultBuffer`, `TryReadCoreVaultBuffer`, and `IsCoreVaultHandle`. Owner-created rows are acquired through `GetGenerationHandle` and resolved phase-locally; existing rows are borrowed through `TryGetGenerationHandle` with exact BufferID, `SystemID.CoreDataVault`, nonzero generation, required length, inactive compaction fence, and `TryResolveHandle` or pure `TryReadHandle`. Mutable job inputs use resolve; config and final readback use read.
Rejected Alternatives: Keeping direct `GetBuffer` because this is the owner path was rejected because owner direct views still set external-view flags. Releasing method-local descriptors was rejected because `GetGenerationHandle` does not create a per-call refcount lease and these are permanent CoreDataVault rows. Rewriting the FrostTick `TryComplete(forceComplete: true)` policy was rejected in this loop because that is dispatcher phase policy, not a Vault pointer route, and changing it without an owner route card could alter simulation timing.
Scalability potential: Low devices retain the continuous quality-weight sweep budget collapse and minimum bounded sweep. Middle/high/ultra retain fuller AUP wrapping, sector-local mirror refresh, and address-shift publication without changing DTO layout, BufferIDs, rollback identity, or CoreDataVault authority. The route scales by cadence/budget, not by binary hardware switches.
Hardware Impact: i3/MX350 pays descriptor validation at prewarm/frost boundaries only. Entity loops still operate over contiguous `NativeArray<T>` views with existing `[NoAlias]` job fields; no per-row descriptor lookup, managed hot-path allocation, DTO stride change, shader variant, direct sibling dependency, or hot GlobalRegistry polling was introduced.
